 - **`g33-stall`** (10 pairs, 512 tokens, `PF2` = team on CPUs 0,2,..,14 against `PF2B` = 2,4,..,14,16): 124.69 against 125.35 tok/s, **+0.53%** (9 of 10 pairs positive, text identical in 10 of 10), engine serve avg per request 90.5 -> 78.2 us, host leg by miss count 46.1 / 78.1 / 115.7 .. 331.2 -> 37.9 / 67.8 / 101.3 .. 287.6 us (per expert 40.2 -> 35.7 us). Requests over 3 ms: `PF2` 2 (both name CPU 0), `PF2B` 0; over 5 ms: none. So **G16-2 and G16-3 held, G16-1 did not hold as written** (its baseline clause asked for at least 5 events in the `PF2` arm; the stall rate of this session was 0.2 per run, against 0.8 per run in the G15 sessions). The anatomy explained the small gain: the stall fell by 314 us per token and the host serve by 313 us, but **the gap between two tokens grew by 230 us (1,350 -> 1,580)**. Hypothesis written down before the next session: with core 0 free of the team, the scheduler places the decoding thread's wake-up after the logits copy on the interrupt core.
 - **`g34-main`** (the same contract repeated with 2,048-token runs for more events, 6 counterbalanced pairs, four arms; build g33 adds `LLAMA_MOE_HYBRID_MAIN_CPU`, which pins the decoding thread at its first step): `PF2` 124.34 tok/s (token 8,029 us: attn 3,651 + overlap 1,483 + stall 1,558, gap 1,338), `PF2B` 125.37 (7,964: stall 1,231, **gap 1,559**), **`PF2BM` = team on 2,4,..,16 + decoding thread on CPU 18: 128.74 (7,754: stall 1,219, gap 1,361)**, `PF2M` = the old team + pinned thread 124.62 (8,011: stall 1,543, gap 1,332). Paired against `PF2`: `PF2B` +0.83% (6 of 6), **`PF2BM` +3.54% (6 of 6; +2.76, +3.20, +4.21, +3.90, +3.82, +3.34)**, `PF2M` +0.23% (4 of 6). Requests over 3 ms: `PF2` 12 and `PF2M` 11, **all 23 name CPU 0**; `PF2B` 0 and `PF2BM` 0; over 5 ms: 1 (`PF2`), none elsewhere. Text identical in every pair. Serve avg per request 90.8 -> 78.2 us.
 - **Criteria.** G16-1 (cause): in `g34-main` the `PF2` arm had 12 requests over 3 ms (bar 5), 12 of 12 naming CPU 0 (bar 90%), and `PF2B` / `PF2BM` had none (bar 1): held; in `g33-stall` the baseline clause failed, as said. G16-2 (no loss): `PF2B` +0.53% / +0.83%, 9 of 10 and 6 of 6 positive: held. G16-3 (text): 16 of 16 pairs identical: held. **ROOT CAUSE of ledger row 27 (the 3-100 ms host stalls): the worker pinned to logical CPU 0.** CPU 0 and its SMT sibling CPU 1 are the machine's interrupt core (CPU 1: 14.2% interrupt + 4.3% DPC time during a run); a thread pinned there is held off for milliseconds (0.6-4.4 ms for 38 of the 39 certified-session lines, 102 ms for the longest, and the 104-105 ms events of row 27 and of G14 / G15), and every barrier of the team then waits for it. The same placement problem hides a second one: once the team leaves core 0, the OS puts the decoding thread's wake-up there and the gap grows by about 230 us unless the thread is pinned. Both are scheduler-placement effects of this machine, not of the engine's logic.
 - **Decision (agent's own, reversible by env): the defaults of the lean team become** the logical CPUs 2,4,..,16 (core 0 left alone; the 8th thread on the other CCD, which also adds a memory path: per expert 40.2 -> 35.7 us) and the decoding thread pinned to the CPU after the team (18); `LLAMA_MOE_HYBRID_LEAN_CPUS` and `LLAMA_MOE_HYBRID_MAIN_CPU` override (-1 = unpinned). Build g34. The graph leg keeps its placement (it is the control). The earlier G14 / G15 results stand for the old placement (they are certified numbers of that configuration); the final-state sessions below re-measure the defaults.

## G17 CONTRACT (agent's own; written 2026-10-12 BEFORE `tools/analyze-policy-replay.py` is run) - the cache policy itself, by exact replay (ledger rows 17, 22, 25)
Origin: rows 22 and 25 were judged from a STATIC coverage argument (22) and from a replay at 42 slots with a different engine (25); row 17 (second-touch admission, co-activation prewarm) from a cache simulation that was never run against the engine's policy. Since G13 the replay of the engine policy is exact (the hit rate of both capture prompts is reproduced to 4 decimals and step by step), so the policy space can be explored offline at the engine's real shape: 81 slots x 40 layers, B = 16, lambda 0.97, scan mode 2 (lag 1), tie-break from the training prior, the g28 captures of the code and prose prompts (511 steps each).
Variants (`tools/analyze-policy-replay.py`): row 22, a per-layer slot split at EQUAL total slots (3,240): proportional to the misses of the OTHER prompt's baseline replay (exponent 0.5 and 1; the honest, non-oracle form), a static greedy split from the training prior alone, and the same-prompt split as the ceiling; rows 17 and 25, the promotion budget B = 8, 12, 24, 32, a minimum gain for a promotion (0.002 .. 0.02), a recency filter (a candidate must have been routed in the latest step, the cheap form of second-touch admission, alone and with B = 24 / 32), and a layer-type dependent EMA decay (gated DeltaNet layers against full-attention layers, six pairs).
Model of the value (a screen, not a measurement): change of the host time per token = -(extra hits per token) x 42 us (the in-situ cost of a cold missed expert on the lean leg) + (extra promotions per token) x 7 us (the DRAM time a promotion's DMA takes from the miss path, ledger row 23).
Criteria (not to be moved after the numbers):
 - G17-0 validity: the baseline replay's hit rate over all steps equals the engine's logged 0.8213 (code) and 0.8371 (prose) to four decimals.
 - G17-1 a variant is WORTH AN ENGINE A/B if its modelled change of host time is at least 120 us per token (1.5% of a 8.0 ms token, about +0.9 pp of hit rate) on BOTH prompts, and for row 22 the non-oracle splits must also reach +0.9 pp of hit rate on both prompts; the oracle split and any variant tuned on the prompt it is scored on are ceilings, never candidates.
 - G17-2 everything else is recorded as BOUNDED BELOW THE NOISE FLOOR of the speed sessions (about 0.5%), with the table as the evidence. A variant that passes G17-1 is implemented in the engine behind an env var and measured with the usual 6-pair session (bar +1.0%, 5 of 6 pairs, text allowed to differ because the cache contents differ; the answer-stability gate runs on the final build).

### G17 RESULTS, offline (`tools/analyze-policy-replay.py`; outputs `build/llama-cuda/g17-policy/` and `g17-policy-combos/`; seconds per replay)
 - **G17-0 PASS**: the baseline replay reproduces the engine's all-steps hit rate on both captures, 0.8213 (code) and 0.8371 (prose), to four decimals.
 - Single variants (hit rate over steps >= 8, baseline 0.8271 code / 0.8387 prose; modelled change of host time per token in brackets, code / prose): the cross-prompt per-layer split (slots proportional to the OTHER prompt's baseline misses ^ 0.5 or ^ 1.0, same 3,240 total slots): **+0.78 / +0.85 pp (-105 / -116 us)** at the best exponent per prompt, +0.60 / +0.58 pp at the other; the same-prompt split (a ceiling) +0.92 / +1.17 pp (-124 / -161 us); the split from the training prior alone +0.28 / +0.50 pp (-37 / -67 us). Budget B = 8 / 12 are clearly worse (-4.72 / -1.39 pp code), B = 24: +0.98 / +1.07 pp (-76 / -102 us), B = 32: +1.32 / +1.58 pp (-71 / -141 us) because the 7 us per extra promotion eats about half of the gain. A gain threshold changes nothing (the budget binds, not the gain), the recency filter alone is neutral (-0.04 / -0.03 pp, 0.8 fewer promotions per token on prose), the layer-type dependent decay (DeltaNet 0.95-0.98 against full-attention 0.90-0.99) never helps (best +0.17 pp on prose, worst -2.47 pp on code). **By G17-1 no single variant reaches 120 us on both prompts**: they are BOUNDED below the bar.
 - Combinations (run because the single results suggested a synergy: more slots where the misses are, a budget big enough to fill them): cross-prompt split ^ 0.5 + B = 24: **+1.64 / +1.57 pp, -165 / -172 us (-2.1 / -2.2%)**; + B = 32: +2.04 / +2.19 pp (-168 / -226 us); + B = 32 + recency: **+2.10 / +1.97 pp, -201 / -208 us (-2.5 / -2.6%)**; split ^ 1.0 + B = 32 gives +1.83 / +2.46 pp (-143 / -266 us). **Every combination with B >= 24 passes G17-1 on both prompts.** These are scored on the held-out prompt (the split comes from the other capture), but they remain a model of the value (the 7 us per promotion; no timeline), so they become candidates for an ENGINE A/B (contract below), not results.

## G17c CONTRACT (agent's own; written 2026-10-12 BEFORE any engine run of the policy variants) - the cache policy variants that passed the offline bar, measured in the engine (ledger rows 17, 22, 25)
Built (build g34; all opt-in env vars, default = the old behaviour): `LLAMA_MOE_HYBRID_SPLIT=<file>` (n_layer weights, here the per-layer misses of the baseline replay of code + prose pooled, `build/llama-cuda/aer-split-misses-pooled.txt`, written by `analyze-policy-replay.py --emit-split`) with `_SPLIT_ALPHA` (default 0.5), `_SPLIT_MIN` / `_SPLIT_MAX` (24 / 160): per-layer cache tensors, per-layer zero slot, offsets into the slot table, the same TOTAL slots as the uniform split so the VRAM is the same and `--tg-hybrid-slots auto` is untouched; `LLAMA_MOE_HYBRID_RECENCY=1`: a promotion candidate must have been routed in the latest ingested step; the budget is the existing `LLAMA_MOE_HYBRID_B`. The same build carries the G16 defaults (team off core 0, decoding thread pinned).
Sessions (specs `g35-pol-code.json`, `g35-pol-prose.json`, `g35-pol-anchor.json`; certified, mains, counterbalanced, 4 reps per arm, 512 tokens; the code and prose calibration is IN-SAMPLE for those two prompts, the anchor prompt `anchor-512-nonl.txt` (511 tokens, prefilled with ubatch 24) is held out): arms `P0` (uniform split, B = 16: the baseline), `P1` (split ^ 0.5, B = 16), `P2` (split ^ 0.5, B = 24), `P3` (split ^ 0.5, B = 32, recency), `P4` (uniform, B = 32, recency); all with `LEAN=1`, `PF=2`, scan mode 2, fast sampler, input batching.
Criteria (not to be moved after the results):
 - G17c-1 candidate: the arm with the largest sum of its mean gains over `P0` on the code and prose sessions is the candidate. It passes if its mean gain is at least +1.0% with at least 3 of 4 pairs positive on BOTH the code and the prose session. (Prediction from the model: `P3` +2.5% / +2.6%, `P2` +2.1% / +2.2%, `P1` +1.3% / +1.0%, `P4` +1.2% / +1.6%; the engine has costs the model does not, so the first measurement may land anywhere from a loss to the model.)
 - G17c-2 held-out: the same arm on the anchor prompt gains at least +0.5% with at least 3 of 4 pairs positive.
 - G17c-3 every run rc 0, check-telemetry 0, guards 0, poller peak at most 8,192 MiB. The generated text may differ between arms (different cache contents); the answer-stability gate (needle QA at 4,096 tokens, negative control failing as designed) runs on the final build with the candidate's settings before the policy is called part of the recommended configuration.
If the candidate fails G17c-1 or G17c-2 the rows stay BOUNDED, with the measured cause (hit rate gained against the extra promotions' DRAM and PCIe cost, read from the engine log's promotions per token, serve time and stall).

### G17c RESULTS (build g34 = `bin/g34`, digest `49F5E94E278D8C64`, llama.dll `9E9B0F4B91EDF037`; certified; mains; `tools/pol-table.py`)
Defaults confirmation first (`g35-def`, 6 pairs, code): the new placement (team off core 0, decoding thread pinned) **129.36 tok/s against the old placement 124.89, +3.58%** (6 of 6, text identical, serve 90.5 -> 77.7 us), requests over 3 ms: 0 against 4 (all CPU 0). Policy sessions (4 counterbalanced reps per arm, 512 tokens, per-layer split ^ 0.5 from the pooled calibration of code + prose; the code and prose columns are IN-SAMPLE, the anchor prompt is held out):
| arm | code tok/s (gain, hit, promotions/token) | prose | anchor (held out) |
|---|---|---|---|
| P0 uniform, B = 16 | 128.86 (-, 0.8213, 16.0) | 131.18 (-, 0.8307, 16.0) | 127.44 (-, 0.8137, 16.0) |
| P1 split, B = 16 | 129.36 (**+0.39%**, 3/4, 0.8265, 16.0) | 132.68 (**+1.15%**, 4/4, 0.8419, 15.8) | 122.92 (**-3.55%**, 0/4, 0.7689, 16.0) |
| P2 split, B = 24 | 128.97 (+0.08%, 2/4, 0.8402, 23.9) | 131.72 (+0.42%, 3/4, 0.8515, 22.2) | 126.59 (-0.67%, 0/4, 0.8242, 19.8) |
| P3 split, B = 32, recency | 128.53 (-0.26%, 1/4, 0.8462, 27.2) | 130.53 (-0.49%, 0/4, 0.8495, 25.0) | 120.76 (-5.24%, 0/4, 0.7904, 30.5) |
| P4 uniform, B = 32, recency | 127.30 (-1.21%, 0/4, 0.8411, 28.0) | 130.54 (-0.49%, 0/4, 0.8516, 24.6) | 123.91 (-2.77%, 0/4, 0.8224, 28.3) |
 - **G17c-1 FAILED**: the candidate is `P1` (largest sum of mean gains, +1.54); it needs +1.0% on both prompts and has +0.39% on code. **G17c-2 FAILED decisively**: on the held-out anchor prompt the same split loses 3.55% (the hit rate falls from 0.8137 to 0.7689) and every other variant loses too. G17c-3 held (rc 0, peak 8,005 / 7,977 MiB). So **the policy rows are CLOSED, not accepted.**
 - Why the offline model was too optimistic (measured, token anatomy of the code session, mean per token, `P0` -> `P3`): the stall fell as predicted (1,183 -> 1,001 us, hit rate +2.5 pp, host serve -239 us) but the GPU's own terms grew: **attention front +177 us, overlap +34 us, result load +54 us, promotion-copy submission +62 us** (`P2`: +125 / +26 / +40 / +49), so the token did not get shorter (+16 us). Each EXTRA promotion costs about **24 us** of GPU and PCIe time (the model had 7 us): the copy engine's bulk reads of host memory delay the GPU's small mailbox reads and writes over the same link (the result load alone rises from 171 to 225 us per token). The ledger's old B sweep (B = 32 -1.8%) was right for the right reason, and B = 16 is near the optimum of this trade. The cross-prompt transfer of the split that the offline study showed (code <-> prose) does not extend to a third prompt: the per-layer miss profile is prompt dependent (a wrong split costs 4.5 pp of hit rate, a right one gains at most 0.9-1.2 pp), so a static split is a gamble; an adaptive one would have to move cache slots between layers at run time (a per-layer reallocation) for a ceiling of about 1% after the prefetch of G15 already removes the cheap misses.

## G18 CONTRACT (agent's own; written 2026-10-12 BEFORE the final sessions) - the final configuration against stock under the owner's own definition of "8 GB"
Configuration under test (build g35: the engine of g34 plus a measurement flag in the runner; every option is in the spec `g37-final-*.json`, none is an env override of a default): the G9 recipe (`--kv-graph-pad 256 --kv-lazy --tg-hybrid-slots auto --tg-vram-cap-mib 0 --tg-vram-keep-free-mib 16000 --tg-release-compute`: the cache is sized so that at least 16,000 MiB stay free in the CUDA runtime's view, which is what the owner's `llama-server --fit-target 16000` asks for) + the accepted options (batch-major prefill with `--ubatch 24`, input batching, the async scan, the fast sampler) + the lean host leg and the K = 2 prefetch (G14, G15) with the team and the decoding thread placed as G16 found. Arms: `STK` (stock `llama-completion` with the owner's flags), `KFB` (the keep-free hybrid with the graph host leg and the old placement: the state before G14), `KFP` (the final configuration), `KFM` (`KFP` + MTP self-speculation with the draft layer's experts on the GPU). Prompts: code and prose, 512 tokens, 4 counterbalanced reps per arm, every hybrid arm watches `cudaMemGetInfo` every 20 ms.
Criteria (not to be moved after the results):
 - G18-1 the owner's definition holds: in every hybrid run the watcher's minimum free device memory is at least 16,000 MiB, and the cache size the runner picked is printed. (G9 got 16,177 MiB with 69 slots for the accepted options of that day.)
 - G18-2 against stock, same session: `KFP` is at least 3.0x the `STK` arm's tok/s on both prompts (the G9 recipe measured 2.74x on the code prompt; the host-leg work since then is worth about +20%).
 - G18-3 against the state before G14: `KFP` is at least 8% faster than `KFB` on both prompts (prediction +11%).
 - G18-4 `KFM` against `KFP`: reported; on the code prompt it must not be slower (the MTP draft layer costs about 7 cache slots under the same keep-free rule).
 - Reported, not gated: the ratios to the owner's document baseline of 30.68 tok/s (llama-server) and to the iso-VRAM `llama-server --fit-target 16000` of G9 (33.8 / 33.0 tok/s, code / prose), the slots each arm got, the poller peaks, and the answer-stability gate (needle QA at 4,096 and 24,000 prompt tokens, negative control failing as designed) for `KFP` and `KFM` on the final build.

### G18 RESULTS (build g35 = `bin/g35`, engine llama.dll `9E9B0F4B91EDF037`; certified sessions `g37-final-code` (digest `C6CE5B7F053E1702`) and `g37-final-prose` (`1C084A7A88C32896`); mains; 4 counterbalanced reps per arm, 512 tokens; the owner's definition of 8 GB: at least 16,000 MiB free in the CUDA runtime's view)
| arm | code tok/s (min-max) | prose tok/s | slots | watcher minimum free | poller peak | x stock (code / prose) | x the document's 30.68 (code / prose) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| STK stock `llama-completion`, the owner's flags | 37.11 (36.5-37.5) | 37.35 | - | not watchable (poller 7,435 MiB: 15,684 MiB free, 316 MiB short) | 7,435 / 7,437 | 1.00 / 1.00 | 1.21 / 1.22 |
| KFB keep-free hybrid, graph host leg, old placement (the state before G14) | 106.02 (105.3-106.4) | 109.24 | 69 | 16,191 MiB | 7,185 | 2.86 / 2.92 | 3.46 / 3.56 |
| **KFP the final configuration** (lean leg + K = 2 prefetch + new placement) | **124.47 (124.2-124.7)** | **127.56** | 69 | 16,191 MiB | 7,185 | **3.35 / 3.42** | **4.06 / 4.16** |
| KFM KFP + MTP (1 draft, draft layer's experts on the GPU) | **135.63 (134.9-136.7)** | 129.22 | 60 | 16,161 MiB | 7,215 | **3.65 / 3.46** | **4.42 / 4.21** |
 - **G18-1 PASS**: in all 24 hybrid runs the watcher's minimum free device memory is 16,191 MiB (KFB, KFP) or 16,161 MiB (KFM), at least 16,000; the runner picked 69 slots (60 with the MTP draft layer, which costs 472 + 63 MiB).
 - **G18-2 PASS**: KFP is 3.35x (code) and 3.42x (prose) the same session's stock (bar 3.0x). **G18-3 PASS**: KFP against KFB +17.4% (code) and +16.8% (prose) (bar +8%; prediction +11%). **G18-4**: KFM against KFP **+9.0%** on code (not slower; the 9 fewer slots cost it hit rate: 0.7517 against 0.7884) and +1.3% on prose.
 - Against the iso-VRAM `llama-server --fit-target 16000` of G9 (33.8 / 33.0 tok/s warm requests, code / prose) the final configuration is 3.68x / 3.87x. Paired gains of KFP over STK, 4 of 4 pairs positive in both sessions; the hit rate of the hybrid arms is 0.7884 (code) / 0.8082 (prose) at 69 slots, against 0.8213 / 0.8307 at the 81 slots of the poller-unit 8,192 MiB cap used by the G14-G17 sessions (which is why those sessions read 129 tok/s).
 - What this certifies: on THIS card and driver, with 16,000 MiB kept free in the runtime's view and a 262,144-token window, the final configuration decodes 124-128 tok/s (135 with MTP on predictable text) where the owner's stock command decodes 37, i.e. 4.1-4.2x the document's llama-server baseline of 30.68. It does not certify a physical 8 GiB card (G8: a card over-commits and pages instead of failing), other prompts' routing, or the answer stability of the new defaults (the needle gate below).

### G18 answer-stability gate on the final build (needle QA, programmatic grading, negative control must fail; sessions `g38-qa4096` and `g38-qa24000`, digest `C6CE5B7F053E1702`, generator `docs/plans/tg-beat-stock-specs/mk_qa.py`)
`QKFP` (the final plain configuration: keep-free auto slots, lazy KV, lean leg + K = 2 prefetch, async scan, fast sampler, input batching, layer-major prefill) and `QKFM` (the same with MTP, batch-major prefill), 128 generated tokens, ctx 262,144. **All four runs PASS the positive grade and the negative control FAILS as designed**: at 4,096 prompt tokens and at 24,000 prompt tokens both arms answer `QX-7741 TR-2918 MK-5501 LD-8834 Paris` (the planted codes and the planted city), poller peaks 7,169-7,183 MiB. The generated code and prose of the speed sessions are coherent and structurally the same programs as stock's (6-gram repetition ratios 0.91-0.92 on the code prompt for every arm, 1.0 on prose for stock, KFB and KFP, 0.77 for KFM, whose text parts company at a near tie and stays coherent). This certifies answer stability on these two prompts, not token identity (the ladder promises none) and not the 50k-200k lengths, which were not run in this round.

### G19 CONTRACT (deep-fill validation of the final configuration; agent's own, written 2026-10-12 BEFORE the first deep run; generators `docs/plans/tg-beat-stock-specs/mk_deep.py`, `mk_qa.py`)
**Why.** Every speed number of G14-G18 comes from prompts of at most 24,000 tokens, while the owner's target is a 262,144-token window and the owner summary lists "contexts above 24k" as unproven. Two things change with the fill. (1) Decode attention reads the live KV; that cost is paid by both engines (T-GPLR-70 measured 228 us per attention layer at 50k live tokens and 558 us at 128k, about 4.4 us per 1,000 tokens per layer, i.e. about 44 us per 1,000 tokens over the 10 attention layers). (2) The lazily committed KV takes device memory, so the keep-free auto sizing leaves fewer cache slots (24k: 25,152 rows = 280 MiB and 65 slots; 200k adds about 1.9 GiB, about 28 slots of 68.27 MiB fewer) and the hit rate falls. Correctness at depth (lazy commit, q8 KV, long positions, the prefetch) was never exercised past 24k on this configuration.
**Outcome.** At 50,000 / 100,000 / 200,000 prompt tokens, ctx 262,144, under the keep-free rule: the final configuration answers correctly, holds the rule, does not fail, and its TG speed against stock is measured.
**Build and flags.** Build g35 only (engine llama.dll `9E9B0F4B91EDF037`); no source edit during these sessions. QA arms QKFP (layer-major prefill, ubatch 512) and QKFM (MTP, batch-major), 128 generated tokens, exactly the flags of `g38-*`, on `needle-<N>.txt` (`gen-needle-prompt.py --target-tokens N --calibrate bin/g35/llama-tokenize.exe`, exact token counts). Speed arms STK (stock `llama-completion`, the owner's flags, `-c 262000`) and DKFP (= QKFP flags and environment), 512 generated tokens, counterbalanced pairs, one code-continuation prompt per length (`deepcode-<N>.txt` = the needle haystack without its instruction line and answer contract, then the code prompt of the short sessions, no trailing newline), the same bytes for both engines.
**Sessions** (each length its own mutex run, one at a time, never a loop over lengths; the wrapper must report `PROVENANCE GATE ... 0 refused`): `g39-qa50000`, `g39-qa100000`, `g39-qa200000`, `g39-spd50000`, `g39-spd100000`, `g39-spd200000`.
**Criteria (falsifiable; none may move after a result).**
- G19-1 answer stability: per length, QKFP and QKFM: check-telemetry OK, positive grade PASS (the four planted codes and the city), negative control FAILS as designed, exit 0. A failure is attributed by running stock on the same prompt before anything is called an engine defect.
- G19-2 keep-free rule: the watcher's minimum free device memory >= 16,000 MiB in every hybrid run of the six sessions.
- G19-3 speed: per length, every DKFP run is faster than the STK run of its pair AND every DKFP run decodes >= 30.68 tok/s (the document's number).
- G19-4 (reported, no bar): per length the slots the auto sizing picked, the hit rate, the PP rate of both engines, the token anatomy (the attention share of the token) so that the slope has a stated cause, and a coherence check of the generated text of the speed arms.
**Expectations (E, written before the runs; not criteria).** Additive attention cost with no overlap (pessimistic for the hybrid, whose CPU wait can hide GPU work): hybrid about 95 / 77 / 55 tok/s and stock about 34 / 32 / 28 tok/s at 50k / 100k / 200k, i.e. a ratio of about 2.8 / 2.4 / 1.95; slots about 61 / 53 / 36.
**Claims the work may make.** Exactly what G19-1..3 demonstrate at these three fills on this machine and these prompts; nothing about other fills, other prompts, a physical 8 GiB card or token identity (the ladder promises answer stability only).
**Handling a miss.** Diagnose, repair where the cause is in this tree, rerun the same session; a crash or a wrong answer is a hard failure and is root-caused before anything else. **Escalation condition:** a cause outside this tree (for example the model failing the retrieval on stock too) is reported as that, not repaired.

### G19 PROGRESS LOG (2026-10-12; extended as the sessions finish; the RESULTS block replaces it)
- `g39-qa50000` (build g35, digest `C6CE5B7F053E1702`, provenance 0 refused): QKFP and QKFM both PASS (positive grade and negative control as designed). QKFP: PP 50,000 tokens at 2,410 tok/s, 61 auto slots (E: 61), minimum free 16,191 MiB; QKFM: 49 slots, 16,161 MiB. G19-1 and G19-2 hold at 50k.
- `g39-qa100000` (build g35): **two misses.** (1) G19-2: QKFP minimum free **15,387 MiB, 613 MiB under the rule, 3.3 s into the watch (the start of the prefill)**: the layer-major prefill holds the act slab (16,384 B/token in f32 = 1,562 MiB at 100k), the lazily committed KV (1,080 MiB), two layer slots (996 MiB), the scheduler compute buffer (1,196 MiB) and the 14-slot initial cache (1,024 MiB) at the same time; the sum reproduces the reading. (2) G19-1: **QKFM answered wrongly** (it continued the filler rows) while QKFP answered correctly on the same prompt.
- **Root cause of the QKFM miss (confirmed by two controlled runs).** `tools/layer-streaming/main.cpp` padded every `--mtp` prompt with the vocabulary's pad token up to a multiple of the ubatch ("mirror requires exact ubatches"). On Qwen that pad is `<|endoftext|>`, so the model answered from a context that ENDED in up to 511 end-of-text tokens: 352 at 100,000 tokens (and 64 at 24,000, 176 at 50,000, which still passed). Evidence: `g39-h1-qa100352` (build g35, prompt of 100,352 tokens = 196 x 512, nothing to pad): QKFM PASS; `g40-qa100000m` (build g36 = the g35 engine DLLs + the fixed runner exe, the SAME 100,000-token prompt as the failing run, `PP: 100000 tokens`): QKFM PASS. The mirror reads `n_tok` rows and already handles a short last ubatch (the first ubatch is one row short), so the padding is now OFF by default (`LLAMA_MTP_PAD_PROMPT=1` restores the old behaviour for an A/B). **Side effect on earlier evidence:** every KFM/MTP speed row of G10-G18 used prompts padded by up to ubatch-1 end-of-text tokens (at most 23 with the ubatch 24 of the speed sessions, ubatch 512 in the QA sessions); the speed numbers stay true for the configuration that was run, but they are re-measured on g36 (see the G18b block when it exists).
- Decision D64: build g36 = bin/g35 with `llama-layer-streaming.exe` replaced by the one built from the fixed `main.cpp` (exe sha256 prefix `7af14d20baf4f506`, llama.dll unchanged `9E9B0F4B91EDF037`); G19 sessions from here on run on g36 (digest `6A6100DDE7BE7068`). The sessions already run on g35 stay as the record of what g35 does, including its two misses. Criteria are not moved.
- **Repair of the keep-free miss, deep variant "QKFD"** (`mk_deep.py qad`; the criterion G19-2 is not moved, the ARM is repaired): act slab bf16 (the tool's own default, half the bytes of the f32 the g38 specs passed), ONE layer slot with `--pp-async-feed 0` (the first try, `g40-qad100000` as launched, was refused loudly: `--pp-async-feed requires --layer-slots >= 2`; quarantined in `build/llama-cuda/quarantine-refused/`), and a 2-slot initial hybrid cache (`LLAMA_MOE_HYBRID=2`; the auto sizing grows it after the prefill anyway). The MTP arm "QKFMD" keeps two layer slots (batch-major prefill reloads every layer per ubatch) and only gets the small initial cache.
- `g40-qad100000` (g36, QKFD, provenance 0 refused): QA PASS with the negative control as designed, PP 100,000 tokens at 1,970 tok/s (the f32 / 2-slot run of g35 had 1,962: a layer's weights are loaded once per prompt, the load is off the critical path at this length), 53 slots, **minimum free 16,217 MiB** (g35: 15,387), TG 67.19 tok/s at 128 tokens. The bf16 slab did not cost the answer.
- `g40-qad200000` (QKFD): QA PASS, PP 200,000 tokens in 144.4 s (1,385 tok/s), 38 slots (E: 36), **minimum free 15,667 MiB, 333 MiB under the rule, again at the start of the prefill.** `g40-qad200000-nl` (+ `--pp-no-logits`): the same memory to the MiB (the reserve already runs with n_outputs_max = 1 under `--tg-first-pp-token`), PP 142.5 s. **Budget model** (reproduces the 100k f32 and the 200k bf16 readings within 52 MiB): free at the start of the prefill = 21,196.5 MiB (observed after the model load) - [initial cache 204.8 + one layer slot 498 + scheduler compute buffer C(ubatch) + act slab 7.8 KiB/token (bf16) + lazily committed KV 10.6 KiB/token]. With C(512) = 1,196 MiB the strict rule holds up to about 182k prompt tokens. Footprint sweep at the 24k prompt (`g40-ubsweep24k`, all QA PASS, free >= 16,209 MiB): ubatch 256: C = 924 MiB, PP 1,966 tok/s; ubatch 128: C = 788 MiB, PP 1,288 tok/s (the g38 ubatch-512 reference had 2,656 tok/s with an f32 slab and two slots). `g40-qad200000-u128`: QA PASS, PP 874 tok/s (-38% against ubatch 512 at 200k), **minimum free 15,895 MiB, still 105 MiB short** (the gain was 228 MiB, not the 408 MiB the buffer shrank: per-chunk state grows with the chunk count).
- Verdict so far, stated plainly: **G19-2 holds at 50k and 100k for the deep variant and does NOT hold during the layer-major prefill at 200k** (333 MiB short at ubatch 512, 105 MiB at ubatch 128). The TG phase holds it at every fill by construction (the auto sizing leaves 16,160 MiB). A configuration that meets the rule at 200k exists in principle (batch-major prefill has no slab; the QKFMD runs will show it) at roughly one eighth of the prefill speed. No smaller repair was found inside the layer-major design (the slab and the KV are 18.4 KiB per prompt token together; the fixed part is 1.9 GiB).

### G20 CONTRACT (decode attention with grouped-query packing; agent's own, written 2026-10-12 after the micro-benchmark and ONE smoke run, BEFORE the acceptance sessions)
**Why.** G19 showed that the decode flash-attention kernel is the cost that grows with the fill: about 55 us per 1,000 live tokens over the 10 attention layers (3.6 ms of attention at a short fill, 14.4 ms at 200k, i.e. 11 of the 18.3 ms of a hybrid token), paid by every engine that decodes with a quantized KV cache. Measured in isolation (`fa-bench`, 10 layers rotated so the L2 is cold, the model's geometry: head_dim 256, 16 query heads over 2 KV heads, q8_0 K and V, one token): the vector kernel of the tree moves 204 GB/s of KV at every length (131 / 273 / 550 / 1,076 us per layer at 24k / 51k / 102k / 205k keys) on a card whose memory does 896 GB/s. Root cause (read from `fattn-vec.cuh`): each of the 8 query heads that share a KV head gets its own CTA (`K += nb12*(head / gqa_ratio)`), so K and V are read, and V dequantized, 8 times, and every key costs a 5-step warp-shuffle reduction per head; the tensor-core kernel that packs the query heads (`ncols2`) is unreachable for quantized KV at one token (`fattn.cu`).
**What.** `ggml/src/ggml-cuda/fattn-gqa8.cuh`, dispatched from `ggml_cuda_flash_attn_ext` when `LLAMA_FATTN_GQA8=1` (default OFF: nothing changes without it) and the shape fits (one token, head_dim 256, q8_0 K and V, 8 query heads per KV head, f16 mask, no sinks / alibi / softcap, KV length a multiple of 256 and at least `LLAMA_FATTN_GQA8_MIN_KV` = 2048). One CTA per (KV head, KV slice): QK is one thread per key (dp4a against the q8_1 Q of all 8 heads, the same Q quantization as the vector kernel), PV is one warp per key (lane = 8 dims, the V row dequantized once for the 8 heads), tiles of 128 keys dealt round-robin like the vector kernel, partial results in the vector kernel's `dst_tmp` / meta layout, `flash_attn_combine_results` finishes. Feasibility and instrumentation results (not acceptance): micro-benchmark `fa-bench` (scratchpad, includes the tree header), candidate vs vector kernel per layer: 4,096 keys 21.1 vs 31.5 us, 24,576 61.4 vs 140.4, 51,200 101.2 vs 270.1, 102,400 184.4 vs 542.2, 204,800 356.5 vs 1,091.4 (3.06x, 625 GB/s of KV); 768 keys equal, 256 keys slower (hence the minimum KV length); relative error against a double-precision reference identical to the vector kernel's (4.7e-3 .. 6.5e-3, the q8_1 quantization of Q), all 16 heads of the candidate against the vector kernel within 2.1e-7 absolute over 4 mask tails including an entirely masked tile; `test-backend-ops` FLASH_ATTN_EXT (new cases hsk 256, 2 KV heads x 8, q8_0, kv 512 / 2048 / 8192) 3/3 OK against the CPU reference with the flag on and off. One smoke run (`g41-qad100000`, build g37, 128 tokens): QA PASS, TG 91.25 tok/s against 67.19 for the vector kernel on the same prompt and slots.
**Builds and sessions.** Build g37 = g36 + the rebuilt `ggml-cuda.dll` (llama.dll and the runner exe unchanged, `9E9B0F4B91EDF037` / `7af14d20baf4f506`); A/B inside one build by the environment variable, so that the only difference is the flag. Sessions on the G19 prompts: answer stability `g41-qad{50000,100000,200000}` (QKFDG), `g41-qa4096` and `g41-qa24000` (QKFPG, the g38 shape with the flag); speed `g41-spd{50000,100000,200000}` (DKFD vs DKFDG, counterbalanced pairs, 512 tokens) and `g41-spd-short` (the short code prompt, the flag must change nothing there).
**Criteria (falsifiable; none may move after a result).**
- G20-1 kernel correctness: `test-backend-ops -o FLASH_ATTN_EXT -p "hsk=256,hsv=256,nh=2,nr23=\[8,1\].*type_K=q8_0"` passes 3 of 3 with `LLAMA_FATTN_GQA8=1 LLAMA_FATTN_GQA8_MIN_KV=0`; the micro-benchmark's all-heads difference to the vector kernel stays below 1e-5 absolute (done, see above, re-run on the final header).
- G20-2 answer stability: needle QA PASS with the negative control failing as designed, flag on, at 4,096 / 24,000 / 50,000 / 100,000 / 200,000 prompt tokens; the keep-free minimum free memory is not worse than the same arm without the flag.
- G20-3 speed: per fill, paired counterbalanced runs of the same build, all pairs positive and the mean gain at least +9% at 49,956 tokens, +17% at 99,956 and +26% at 199,956 (half of the gain the micro-benchmark predicts through 10 layers: +18% / +34% / +53%).
- G20-4 nothing lost where it does not apply: on the short code prompt (the KV never reaches 2,048 keys) the flag changes TG speed by less than 1.5% either way.
**Claims the work may make.** The kernel is faster by the measured amount for this shape on this GPU, produces answers that pass the needle gate and agrees with the vector kernel to float rounding; nothing about other head sizes, KV types, GQA ratios or GPUs (they fall back to the vector kernel), and nothing about token identity (the summation order differs, text can differ at near-ties).
**Handling a miss.** Diagnose, repair in this kernel, rerun the same session; a wrong answer or a crash is root-caused before anything else.

### G20 RESULTS (build g37 = g36 + rebuilt `ggml-cuda.dll` sha256 prefix `7853bc2b23aa076e`; llama.dll `9E9B0F4B91EDF037` and runner exe `7af14d20baf4f506` unchanged; certified sessions `g41-*`, provenance 0 refused in all; mains; the A/B differs ONLY by `LLAMA_FATTN_GQA8=1`)
| fill | DKFD (vector kernel) tok/s | DKFDG (GQA8) tok/s | gain | positive pairs | bar | attention front `attn` per token | text |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| short code prompt (KV below 2,048 keys, `g41-spd-short`, 4 reps) | 124.31 (123.95-124.58) | 123.99 (123.14-124.66) | **-0.25%** | 2 of 4 | within 1.5% | 3.67 ms both | **identical in 4 of 4 pairs** |
| 49,956 tokens (`g41-spd50000`, 3 reps) | 90.31 (89.98-90.61) | 112.01 (111.93-112.05) | **+24.0%** | 3 of 3 | +9% | 6.40 -> 4.41 ms | differs |
| 99,956 tokens (`g41-spd100000`, 3 reps) | 71.14 (70.75-71.41) | 98.94 (98.58-99.36) | **+39.1%** | 3 of 3 | +17% | 9.03 -> 5.17 ms | differs |
| 199,956 tokens (`g41-spd200000`, 2 reps) | 48.66 (48.63-48.69) | 80.02 (79.85-80.19) | **+64.4%** | 2 of 2 | +26% | 14.31 -> 6.57 ms | differs |

A first run of the flag crashed in the backend-ops test (`ggml-cuda.cu:687 GGML_ASSERT(ptr == pool_addr + pool_used)`): the wrapper allocated the pool buffers in the opposite order of the declaration, the pool is a stack; fixed (allocate in declaration order), the benchmark had not caught it because it allocates by hand.
 - **G20-2 PASS**: needle QA with the flag on at 4,096 / 24,000 / 50,000 / 100,000 / 200,000 tokens (`g41-qa4096`, `g41-qa24000`, `g41-qad50000`, `g41-qad100000`, `g41-qad200000`): positive grade PASS and negative control failing as designed in all five; minimum free memory unchanged against the same arm without the flag (16,193 / 16,197 / 16,191 / 16,217 / 15,667 MiB, the last one is the G19-2 prefill miss, not a property of the flag).
 - **G20-3 PASS** at every fill, and above the bars by a wide margin; the micro-benchmark had predicted +18% / +34% / +53% from the kernel in isolation; why the model gains more than that is not established (candidates: the vector kernel's single-wave launch tail and its interaction with the neighbouring kernels of the graph). **G20-4 PASS**: below 2,048 keys the flag changes nothing (identical text, -0.25%, inside the run-to-run noise of 0.3%).
 - The depth-dependent part of the decode attention (what `attn` grows over its 3.66 ms at a short fill) falls from 2.7 / 5.4 / 10.7 ms to 0.75 / 1.5 / 2.9 ms at 50k / 100k / 200k, a 3.6x cut; the kernel itself reaches about 750 GB/s of KV in the model (micro-benchmark 625 GB/s, the memory does 896).
 - **Against the stock runs of G19 (cross-session, same prompts, same machine state; stock does not use this kernel):** 112.01 / 34.18 = **3.28x** at 50k, 98.94 / 31.40 = **3.15x** at 100k, 80.02 / 26.42 = **3.03x** at 200k (against 2.64x / 2.27x / 1.84x with the vector kernel). The document's baseline of 30.68 tok/s is beaten 2.6x at 200k.
 - What the flag does NOT change: MTP verify steps (two tokens) and every other shape still run the vector kernel; the prefill attention is a different kernel. The recommended recipe gains `LLAMA_FATTN_GQA8=1`.
 - Known limits: head_dim 256, q8_0 K and V, GQA ratio 8, one token, an f16 mask; KV length a multiple of 256 and at least 2,048; other GPUs untested (the kernel uses dp4a, about 200 registers, 39.7 KB of static shared memory, two CTAs per SM on this GPU).

### G21 CONTRACT (the final configuration on a DEDICATED GPU; agent's own, written 2026-10-12 after the owner's two directions below, after the instrumentation sweeps `g45-*` and after the QA sessions `g47-qa4096` .. `g47-qa200000` had run, and before ANY headline result was read. Timing, stated plainly: the queue was launched with the QA and headline sessions together, so `g47-fin50000` had already STARTED (13:04) when this block reached the file (13:05); its output was not read before the text below was fixed, and the criteria are the G19 criteria applied to the final configuration; generators `docs/plans/tg-beat-stock-specs/mk_g45.py`, `mk_g47.py`)
**Owner directions (2026-10-12, chat).** (1) "Use two layer slots for the prefill, there should be enough VRAM." (2) "Assume the GPU will be dedicated to the process and no others will run." **Decision D65 (reading of the keep-free rule).** The owner's `--fit-target 16000` rule (at least 16,000 MiB free in the CUDA runtime's view) describes the memory the decode engine leaves to others. With a dedicated GPU, nothing else needs that memory while the prompt is prefilled, so the rule is read in the DECODE phase: after the prefill's weight slots, act slab and scheduler buffers are released and the decode cache is resized (the runner's free-watch prints a second line for exactly this window). This is a new contract, not a moved criterion: G19-2 keeps its wording (whole run) and its recorded miss at 200k (333 MiB short); G21-2 below is the decode-phase reading. Not reversible after the numbers are read; the whole-run minimum is reported next to it in every row so that either reading can be applied.
**What changed in the prefill (instrumentation, `g45-*`, measured before the QA sessions).** Two layer slots with the async feed, ubatch 2048, f32 slab, on the PP-only 4,096 / 50,000 / 200,000-token prompts: PP 4,096 tokens 1,769 tok/s (ubatch 2048) against 1,553 (ubatch 512), 50,000 tokens 3,050 against 2,428, 200,000 tokens 1,581 against 1,368; ubatch 4096 and 8192 add nothing at 50k (3,062 / 2,918) and cost 2-6 GB of free memory during the prefill. The prefill cost per layer is the copy of one 432 MiB bank over PCIe Gen4 x8 (14.2 GB/s from a pinned buffer, about 11 GB/s from the registered mmap): 32-41 ms per bank, 1.3-1.7 s per prompt whatever its length, so the layer-major prefill is copy-bound up to a few thousand tokens and compute-bound above (see G22).
**Outcome.** The final configuration (layer-major prefill: ubatch 2048, 2 layer slots with the async feed, f32 slab, 14-slot initial cache; decode: auto-sized cache under the keep-free rule, lazy KV, lean host leg + K = 2 prefetch, fast sampler, input batching, GQA8 decode attention; exact flags in `mk_g47.py`, arm `QKFE` / `DKFE`) on build g40, ctx 262,144: answers correctly at 4,096 .. 250,000 prompt tokens, holds the decode-phase rule, and decodes faster than stock at 49,956 / 99,956 / 199,956 prompt tokens.
**Build.** g40 only (g39 engine DLLs, `ggml-cuda.dll` `0cf7d5e39e07286b`, `llama.dll` `9E9B0F4B91EDF037`, runner exe `45274d133771bc88` = g36's plus the decode-phase free-memory watcher); no source edit between the first and the last session of G21 (the provenance gate refuses a session whose binary predates a changed source).
**Sessions** (each one explicit mutex run, never a loop over lengths; the wrapper must report `PROVENANCE GATE ... 0 refused`): QA `g47-qa4096`, `g47-qa24000`, `g47-qa50000`, `g47-qa100000`, `g47-qa200000`, `g47-qa250000` (arm QKFE, 128 generated tokens, needle prompts; 250,000 + 128 fit ctx 262,144); speed `g47-fin50000`, `g47-fin100000`, `g47-fin200000` (arms STK = the owner's `llama-completion` flags, DKFE = QKFE flags and environment, 512 generated tokens, counterbalanced pairs, 3 / 3 / 2 reps, the `deepcode-<N>.txt` prompts of G19, the same bytes for both engines).
**Criteria (falsifiable; none may move after a result).**
- G21-1 answer stability: per length, check-telemetry OK, positive grade PASS (the four planted codes and the city), negative control FAILS as designed, exit 0. A failure is attributed by running stock on the same prompt before anything is called an engine defect.
- G21-2 keep-free rule, decode phase: the decode-phase minimum free device memory (second line of the free-watch report) is at least 16,000 MiB in every hybrid run of the nine sessions. The whole-run minimum is reported, with no bar.
- G21-3 speed: per length, every DKFE run is faster than the STK run of its pair AND every DKFE run decodes at least 30.68 tok/s (the document's number).
- G21-4 (reported, no bar): per length the slots the auto sizing picked, the hit rate, the PP rate and PP time of both engines, the token anatomy, the whole-run minimum free memory, a coherence check of the generated text.
**Expectations (E, written before the headline sessions; not criteria).** The decode side is the G20 configuration with a prefill that leaves the same decode cache (60 / 52 / 37 slots at 50k / 100k / 200k, seen in the QA sessions): DKFE about 110 / 97 / 78 tok/s against stock about 34 / 31 / 26, i.e. ratios about 3.2 / 3.1 / 2.9; PP about 3,000 / 2,400 / 1,600 tok/s (QA sessions: 3,082 / 2,378 / 1,596) against stock about 475 / 463 / 440.
**Claims the work may make.** Exactly what G21-1..3 demonstrate at these lengths on this machine, on these prompts, with the decode-phase reading of the keep-free rule (D65). NOT claimed: the whole-run reading of the rule at 100k and above (the prefill minimum free is 14,253 MiB at 100,000 tokens and 11,651 MiB at 200,000 in the QA sessions), a physical 8 GiB card, other prompts, other GPUs, token identity.
**Handling a miss.** Diagnose, repair where the cause is in this tree, rerun the same session; a crash or a wrong answer is a hard failure and is root-caused first. **Escalation condition:** a cause outside this tree (the model failing the retrieval on stock too) is reported as that, not repaired.

### G22 STUDY (owner request of 2026-10-12: from which prompt length does the layer-major prefill beat the stock prefill, and can the CPU and the GPU share a short prefill? Instrumentation, measurement and model; agent's own; NO acceptance claim; sessions `g46-*`, `g49-*`, `g53-*`, `g54-nsys-pp`, all build g40, all certified)
**The owner's ideas (chat, paraphrased).** (a) The layer-major prefill streams every layer through the card, which is a waste for a prompt of three words: find the smallest prompt for which it beats the stock prefill. (b) After a request, preload the first two layers so that the next request starts at once. (c) Run the short ones on the CPU instead of copying layers. (d) Better: two processing units, one slow (the CPU) and one fast (the GPU): prepare work for the fast one and, while it is not ready, run layers on the slow one; hide the copy of later layers behind CPU work; how much that pays depends on the CPU's time per layer and on the delays of the preparation.

**Measured prefill time in ms (medians of 2 counterbalanced reps; prompts of the needle generator cut to N tokens; 8 generated tokens, only the prefill is quoted).** STK = the owner's `llama-completion` flags (`-ncmoe 37 -t 16 --no-mmap -fa on -ctk q8_0 -ctv q8_0`, default ubatch 512, op offload on); STKNO = the same with `--no-op-offload`; STKU = the same with `-ub 2048` (all three from `g49-*`, one session per length). LM cold = the layer-major prefill of the final configuration (ubatch 2048, two slots with the async feed, f32 slab) in a fresh process (`g46-*`); LM warm = the same, second in-process pass (`g53-*`, `--pp-repeat 2`). llama.cpp warms up before its timer, a fresh layer-major process does not (row 57), so the warm column is the like-for-like one.

| prompt tokens | STK | STKNO (CPU) | STKU (ub 2048) | LM cold | LM warm |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 3 | 82 | 96 | 91 | 1,400 | - |
| 10 | 146 | 157 | 167 | 1,551 | - |
| 14 | 175 | 215 | 190 | 1,435 | - |
| 29 | 347 | 356 | 345 | 1,756 | - |
| 63 | 726 | **485** | 725 | 1,694 | 1,336 |
| 125 | 904 | **711** | 903 | 1,553 | - |
| 256 | 1,113 | 1,143 | 1,113 | 1,659 | 1,391 |
| 511 | **1,248** | 1,992 | 1,250 | 1,836 | 1,429 |
| 1,023 | 2,314 | 3,913 | **1,383** | 1,690 | 1,501 |
| 2,047 | 4,496 | 8,912 | **1,572** | 1,901 | 1,692 |
| 4,096 | (not run) | - | (not run) | 2,350 | 2,126 |

**Answer to (a), measured.** Against the owner's flags the layer-major prefill is slower up to about **760 prompt tokens** when it starts cold and up to about **600** when both engines are warm (interpolation between 511 and 1,023 tokens: stock 1,248 + 2.08 ms per token, layer-major warm 1,429 + 0.14 ms per token), and faster above (2.4x at 2,047 tokens, 6.4x at 50,000). **Against stock with ubatch 2048 the crossover is one ubatch, about 2,100 tokens** (STKU is faster than the warm layer-major prefill at 1,023 and 2,047 tokens; above 2,048 stock needs a second ubatch and a second copy of the used experts, estimated 2.9-3.1 s at 3,000 tokens against 1.85 s: inferred, not run). The warm layer-major time is flat in the prompt: 1.34 s at 63 tokens, 1.43 s at 511, 1.69 s at 2,047.

**Root cause 1: what the layer-major prefill pays (the floor).** All 40 banks (432 MiB each, 16.9 GiB) cross PCIe Gen4 x8 once per prompt. In the profile of a 63-token prefill (`g54-nsys-pp`, nsys) a bank is three copies of 144 MiB at 10.5 ms each, i.e. **31.5 ms per bank at 14.4 GB/s**; 40 x 31.5 ms = 1.26 s, and the warm measurement is 1.34 s. The 2,047-token traces fit 41 ms per bank (the layers' compute seems to slow the DMA by ~25% at that length; unexplained). The GPU time of a layer with a resident bank is 0.7 ms (recurrent layer, 3 tokens) to 9.3 ms (2,047 tokens), always hidden behind the copy.
**Root cause 2: what the stock prefill pays (mechanism, measured by `g49-*`).** For a batch of 32 tokens or more the scheduler runs the expert matmuls of the 37 CPU layers on the GPU and copies the experts that the batch uses over the same link (`ggml-backend.cpp`: "copy only the experts that are used"), once per 512-token ubatch. With the offload switched off (`STKNO`) the same engine computes the experts on the CPU and the time changes exactly as the CPU micro-benchmark predicts (below): 485 against 726 ms at 63 tokens (**1.50x faster**), 711 against 904 at 125 (1.27x), equal at 256, 1.6-2.0x slower at 511 and above. The arithmetic "37 layers x used fraction of a bank x 31.9 ms" with the routing skew of the benchmark (59% / 81% / 92% / 99% of the experts used at 63 / 125 / 256 / 511 tokens) gives 751 / 1,009 / 1,134 / 1,216 ms against the measured 726 / 904 / 1,113 / 1,248 (within 12%), and per ubatch beyond (2,314 and 4,496 ms at 1,023 and 2,047 tokens are two and four ubatches). So the stock prefill IS a PCIe copy of the used experts per ubatch; below 32 tokens it computes on the CPU. Its crossover with the layer-major prefill is where re-copying the used experts every 512 tokens costs more than copying every bank once.
**Root cause 3: the CPU path in isolation** (`tools/moe-cpu-batch-bench.cpp`: ONE layer's MoE FFN, 256 experts of 2048 x 512, 8 used, Q4_K, ggml-cpu `mul_mat_id`, 16 threads, cold DRAM, no GPU): 1 / 8 / 32 / 64 / 128 / 256 / 512 / 1,024 / 2,048 tokens take 0.24 / 1.9 / 6.5 / 10.5 / 17.5 / 32.2 / 50.1 / 97.9 / 189.7 ms. 37 layers times the benchmark's per-layer time with the skewed routing of the real prompts (9.0 / 15.9 / 28.9 / 52.9 ms at 64 / 128 / 256 / 512 tokens) give 334 / 588 / 1,071 / 1,957 ms against the STKNO times 485 / 711 / 1,143 / 1,992; the remainders (151, 123, 72, 35 ms) are the dense part and the three layers whose experts sit on the GPU, and 1,023 tokens (two ubatches of 52.9 x 37) is 3,914 against 3,913 measured. The benchmark is therefore the right SIZE for the CPU leg of an engine that really runs it, not an exact model of it (at 2,047 tokens the measured 8,912 ms is 14% above four such ubatches).
**Root cause 4: why an attention layer costs a fixed ~32 ms in the layer-major prefill (ledger row 56).** The profile joins each memcpy to its API call: nine 256 KiB host-to-device input copies of the compute stream (positions, KV indices: only attention layers have them) start exactly 31.5 ms after they were submitted, when the 453 MB bank copy that was running on the copy stream ends. The copy engine runs commands in order; a small copy queued behind a bank waits for it. Free while the prefill is copy-bound, 32 ms per attention layer (10 per prompt) as soon as a layer must not wait for the DMA, i.e. exactly in a CPU/GPU interleave. Untested fix: submit banks in pieces of 2-4 MiB.
**Root cause 5: the first pass of a fresh process is 0.2-0.4 s slower** (layer 0 computes in 186-200 ms against 9 ms for the layers after it: one-time kernel loading; plus first-touch costs), and deeper slot windows only recover part of it: warm times with 2 / 4 / 6 / 8 slots are 1,336 / 1,331 / 1,335 / 1,334 ms at 63 tokens and 1,692 / 1,650 / 1,652 / 1,650 at 2,047 (-2.5%), 2,126 / 2,042 / 2,044 / 2,042 at 4,096 (-4.0%). Two slots are enough for a warm prefill (ledger row 57).

**The schedule model** (`tools/g22-model.py` v3, host-only). Inputs: the per-layer GPU times of the sweep traces by layer type (recurrent 0.7-9.3 ms, attention 32.5-38.9 ms), the copy time per bank fitted from the traces (33-41 ms), the CPU table above, a hidden-state round trip, the DRAM contention measured by `dram-share` (a 14.2 GB/s copy raises a CPU layer's cost by 16.3%), and the controller's slot protocol (slot = layer % S, the first streamed layer of each slot submitted at t = 0, the successor submitted when the slot's layer ends, one copy engine). The assignment of the 40 layers to "streamed" or "CPU leg" is SEARCHED (local search from 25 starts), so each cell is the best static plan. Cold-start artefacts (the 190 ms of layer 0) are not in the model. Predicted ms (S = slots, (n) = streamed layers):

| prompt tokens | all-GPU model | best hybrid (S, n) | gain over the all-GPU model | best EXISTING path measured | hybrid against it | hybrid if the attention stall were gone |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 32 | 1,520 | 569 (S6, 14) | 2.7x | STK 347 (29 tokens) | 0.61x | 313 (1.11x) |
| 64 | 1,432 | 653 (S6, 17) | 2.2x | STKNO 485 | 0.74x | 423 (1.15x) |
| 128 | 1,396 | 780 (S6, 21) | 1.8x | STKNO 711 | 0.91x | 584 (1.22x) |
| 256 | 1,474 | 970 (S4, 26) | 1.5x | STK 1,113 | **1.15x** | 805 (1.38x) |
| 512 | 1,595 | 1,157 (S6, 28) | 1.4x | STK 1,248 | **1.08x** | 1,021 (1.22x) |
| 1,024 | 1,516 | 1,294 (S6, 34) | 1.2x | STKU 1,383 | **1.07x** | 1,194 (1.16x) |
| 2,047 | 1,679 | 1,560 (S6, 37) | 1.1x | STKU 1,572 | 1.01x | 1,515 (1.04x) |

(The "all-GPU model" is the model's own streamed-only plan with the best slot count; the measured warm layer-major times are 1,336 / 1,391 / 1,429 / 1,501 / 1,692 at 63 / 256 / 511 / 1,023 / 2,047 tokens, i.e. the model is 7% / 6% / 12% high / 1% high / 1% low: conservative at small N because it uses the copy times fitted to the cold traces.) Sensitivity of the best hybrid against the better of stock and the all-GPU model at 64 / 256 / 1,024 / 2,047 tokens (base 1.12x / 1.15x / 1.17x / 1.08x): copy time 32 ms (the pinned rate) 1.14x / 1.22x / 1.14x / 1.00x; CPU layers 1.4x slower and the copy 1.15x slower while they overlap 1.02x / 1.01x / 1.17x / 1.06x; MoE share of a recurrent layer 0.8 instead of 0.5 within 2% of the base.

**What the study says about the owner's ideas.** (a) answered above. (b) Preloading K banks saves K x 32-41 ms: 82 ms for two banks = 6% of the 1.34 s floor, and the preloaded banks must stay on the card through the decode, i.e. cost decode cache slots (a bank is 6.3 slots): BOUNDED, not built (ledger row 53). (c) is what the stock engine does for fewer than 32 tokens and, with `--no-op-offload`, for up to ~200: **a policy of "CPU path below ~200 tokens, offload path with ubatch 2048 up to ~2,100, layer-major above" is available today and is measured** (the best-existing column above). (d) The interleave is real but small: +8-17% over the better of the stock offload path and the all-GPU model at 64-2,047 tokens, +1-15% over the best EXISTING path measured in the 256-2,047 band, and it LOSES to the CPU path below 128 tokens, because every attention layer waits for the in-flight bank (root cause 4); with that stall gone the model gives 1.15-1.38x over the best existing path at 64-1,024 tokens. The CPU is a useful partner only for the recurrent layers (30 of 40) and only where the DMA does not block the layer's small copies.
**Prototype plan (stages, each with its contract before its first run).** A: the runner can run the prompt through the stock graph path (the same decode path the TG phase uses, with the context's ubatch 2048) when the prompt is shorter than a threshold, with the scheduler's op offload set by length: this realises (c) inside the final configuration. B: submit bank copies in 2-4 MiB pieces and re-measure the attention layers (the test of root cause 4; neutral or positive for the all-GPU prefill). C: `--pp-cpu-layers` in the layer-major controller (written and syntax-checked in a scratch copy; applied only after the last g40 session): the CPU leg of (d), judged by the model's predictions with B in place.

### G23 PROBE (exploratory, BATTERY power, 2026-10-12 19:20; `tools/ce-hol-bench`, output `build/llama-cuda/ce-hol-1.txt`, wrapper verdict `ce-hol-1`, provenance gate 0 refused): can a bank copy be submitted so that a small copy does not wait for it?
**Question (ledger row 56).** A 256 KiB host-to-device input copy on the compute stream starts only when the bank copy on the copy stream ends (root cause 4). **Method.** One 432 MiB bank (pinned source, three 144 MiB members) is copied on stream A; 3,000 us after it starts a 256 KiB copy is submitted on stream B and timed from submission to completion on the host (9 trials, medians; pageable and pinned sources, B also at the highest stream priority). Variants: the three members as three commands (what the runner does), one 432 MiB command, pieces of 1-32 MiB all enqueued at once, and pieces of 2-16 MiB with at most W in flight (the issuing thread waits for the event of piece i-W before issuing piece i).
| variant | bank copy alone, ms (GB/s) | 256 KiB copy during the bank, us (pageable) |
|---|---|---|
| 3 x 144 MiB (today) | 31.77 (14.26) | **31,523** |
| 1 x 432 MiB | 31.78 (14.25) | 31,562 |
| pieces 1 / 2 / 4 / 8 / 16 / 32 MiB, all enqueued at once | 32.98 / 32.56 / 32.21 / 31.98 / 31.88 / 31.83 | 30,192 / 31,933 / 31,762 / 31,674 / 31,611 / 31,592 |
| paced 2 MiB x 2 in flight | 37.99 (11.92) | 243 |
| paced 2 MiB x 4 | 35.16 (12.88) | 541 |
| paced 4 MiB x 2 / x 3 / x 4 | 35.02 / 34.17 / 34.11 | 543 / 732 / 1,015 |
| paced 8 MiB x 2 / x 3 | 33.54 / 33.10 | 1,030 / 1,538 |
| **paced 16 MiB x 2** | **32.57 (13.91)** | **2,122** |
The 256 KiB copy alone takes 70 us. Pinned-source copies and the highest stream priority change nothing (same figures). **Reading.** (1) The stall is reproduced exactly (31.5 ms = the bank). (2) Chunking alone does nothing: all pieces sit in the queue ahead of the small copy. (3) Pacing works: the small copy waits for about W pieces. (4) Pacing costs one host round trip per piece (about 30 us: 216 pieces of 2 MiB cost +20% of the bank copy, 27 pieces of 16 MiB cost +2.5%); the trade is latency (about (W-0.5) x piece / 14.2 GB/s) against copy rate. The design default is 16 MiB x 2 (2.1 ms, +2.5%). Battery power changes absolute rates (the machine's "ac_line" was `battery`, GPU power cap Active); the ORDER property measured here does not depend on them, but the table is exploratory and is re-run on mains inside the contract below.
**What the warm all-GPU prefill does with the stall (trace `g53-63-LM2W_r1`, pass 2, 63 tokens).** Banks complete every 32.9-33.2 ms (1,332 ms for 40 banks) against the 31.8 ms of the probe: the pipeline is copy-bound; the ten attention layers show `layer_compute` = 33 ms (the stall) and every recurrent layer 1.2-1.5 ms, and the stall is hidden behind the next bank. So pacing cannot speed up the all-GPU prefill; it exists for the CPU leg, where the small copy that matters is the result of a CPU layer (and the next attention layer's inputs).

### G23 CONTRACT (the G22 prototype, stages A, B, C; agent's own, written 2026-10-12 evening BEFORE the first GPU run of any new flag; build g41 = the g40 sources + three opt-in edits; generators `docs/plans/tg-beat-stock-specs/mk_g50.py` (C) and `mk_g54.py` (A, B); battery power until further notice, see D74)
**Observable outcome.** Three opt-in runner flags, each default off: **A** `--pp-stock-below N [--pp-stock-offload 0|1]` (a prompt shorter than N tokens is decoded through the stock graph path: experts in host memory, attention on the GPU, the scheduler's op offload on or off), **B** `--pp-copy-piece-mib P [--pp-copy-window W]` (the async-feed worker issues each 144 MiB member in P MiB pieces with at most W in flight, releasing the shared mutex while it does so; the main thread's preload stays unpaced), **C** `--pp-cpu-layers L|all` (the listed layers run their routed experts on the CPU from the host banks in the layer-major prefill: no slot, no copy, no consumer event; the feed skips them; op offload off; CPU threads set to the batch count). Flags absent: the runner is build g40's runner (B4/A4 below). All sessions use the final configuration's flags (ubatch 2048, f32 slab, lazy KV, GQA8, keep-free auto slots, ctx 262,144, q8 KV), prompts `sweep-<N>.txt` of g46, 8 generated tokens, same-session counterbalanced pairs.
**Claims the work may make.** Only those a criterion below defines: the flags work and are exact where C-numbers/A-numbers say so; the paced copy removes the small-copy stall at the stated cost; the CPU/GPU interleave beats (or does not beat) the best existing path at each tested length. No quality claim beyond the needle gate and the logit/token comparisons named here; no claim about any CPU other than this machine's.
**Criteria (falsifiable; a criterion changes only as a recorded decision made before the rerun).**
- **B1 (exactness, function).** Paced runs (16 MiB x 2 and 4 MiB x 3) of the all-GPU layer-major prefill at 63, 255 and 1,023 tokens print the same `PP-logits first greedy ID` and generate the same 8 token IDs as the unpaced run of the same session; the trace's `async_feed_submit` events carry `pieces` >= 3 and `pace_wait_us` > 0 on every worker-fed bank (pacing was active, not silently off).
- **B2 (mechanism).** In the paced trace of the warm pass at 63 tokens every attention layer's `layer_compute` is <= 10 ms where the unpaced trace of the same session shows ~33 ms, and recurrent layers stay within +-2 ms.
- **B3 (cost).** Warm prefill wall of the paced all-GPU run at 63 and 1,023 tokens is within +6% of the unpaced run (probe: +2.5% of the copy rate). A larger cost is root-caused from the per-bank period (`copy_complete` spacing) and repaired by another piece/window; the bound stays.
- **B4 / A4 (default-off identity).** Build g41 without any of the new flags against build g40 (same session, both attested, `exe` per arm): warm prefill wall at 63 and 1,023 tokens within +-2% and the 8 generated tokens identical.
- **A1 (function).** `--pp-stock-below 4096` runs at 3, 14, 29, 63, 125, 256, 511, 1,023 and 2,047 tokens complete with exit 0, print the `pp-stock:` line, generate 8 tokens, and the PP phase summary carries the stock path's wall.
- **A2 (equivalence with the stock binary).** The runner's stock-path prefill wall is within +20% / -10% of the stock binary's prompt-eval wall measured in the same session with the matching flags (runner op offload on, ubatch 512 against `llama-completion` default; op offload off against `--no-op-offload`; op offload on, ubatch 2048 against `-ub 2048`), at 29, 63, 125, 256, 511, 1,023 and 2,047 tokens. A larger deviation is root-caused (runner overhead, scheduler buffer reallocation, graph reuse) and repaired or recorded as a limit.
- **A3 (answer gate).** The needle QA smoke tier (512 tokens, `tools/semantic-qa`, positive pass and negative control failing as designed) passes through `--pp-stock-below 4096`. Token identity with the layer-major run is reported, not gated (the runner's TG phase conditions on a re-decoded last prompt token; the ladder promises answer stability, not token identity).
- **C1 (host proof).** `test-layer-streaming-cpu-leg` and the ten other layer-streaming host tests pass on the rebuilt tree (`ctest -C Release -R "test-layer-streaming-(cpu-leg|descriptors|expert-split|host-mem|layout|model-inspect|route-topk|tel-mark|tg-predict|tg-spec|tg-view)$"`); expected 11/11.
- **C2 (function).** `--pp-cpu-layers all` (HYC) and the model's plans for 2 and 4 slots (HY2, HY4) complete with exit 0 at 63, 255, 511, 1,023 and 2,047 tokens; the telemetry shows no worker-fed bank for HYC, and exactly as many streamed banks as GPU layers in the plan for HY2 / HY4; the runner prints `pp-cpu-layers:`.
- **C3 (exactness).** The CPU leg computes the experts with ggml-cpu `mul_mat_id`, the GPU leg with the CUDA kernels, so bitwise equality is NOT expected. HYC, HY2 and HY4 generate the same 8 token IDs as the all-GPU run of the same session at 255 and 1,023 tokens, or differ only at a step whose all-GPU top-1/top-2 margin is < 0.2 (a near-tie; `--tg-logit-margin 8` prints it); and the needle QA smoke tier passes with `--pp-cpu-layers` = the 512-token HY2 plan (strict gate).
- **C4 (the performance claim).** At each of 255, 511, 1,023 and 2,047 tokens the claim "the CPU/GPU interleave beats the best existing path" is made only if the best of HY2 / HY4 has a prefill wall < 0.97 x min(STK, STKNO, STKU, LM2 warm) in BOTH counterbalanced reps of that session; otherwise the result is "not demonstrated" and C5 applies. The model (`tools/g22-model.py` v3) is judged separately: its prediction for HY2 / HY4 is within 15% of the measured wall, or the term that misses is named.
- **C5 (root cause).** Wherever C4 is "not demonstrated" or the model misses by more than 15%, the dominant term is named from the telemetry of the session (copy-engine idle fraction from `copy_submit`/`copy_complete`, CPU layer time against the benchmark table, the result-copy stall, DRAM contention) and, if it is a defect of the implementation, repaired and the same session rerun before the idea is dismissed.
**Acceptance rule.** A is accepted when A1-A4 hold, B when B1-B4 hold, C when C1-C3 hold and C4/C5 are recorded (C is then accepted as built and measured; the claim is only what C4 says). Timing criteria (A2, B3, B4, A4, C4) are judged on mains power only; on battery a session proves function and exactness (A1, A3, B1, B2, C1-C3) and nothing about speed (the machine's own record: the same flags ran 108.5 tok/s on mains and 49.3 on battery).
**Validation commands.** (1) `cmake --build build/llama-cuda --config Release --target llama-layer-streaming <the eleven test targets> --parallel 12`; (2) the ctest line of C1; (3) smoke runs at 63 tokens of each flag, one at a time under `tools/with-gpu-lock.ps1` with the runner named last on `--attest`; (4) the sessions: `python docs/plans/tg-beat-stock-specs/mk_g54.py build/llama-cuda/bin/g41` (A, B, identity) and `mk_g50.py` (C), run with `tools/run-ab-session.ps1`; (5) the QA gates with `mk_qa.py`; (6) tables with `tools/g22-sweep-table.py`, `tools/g22-hybrid-table.py`, `tools/g22-logit-compare.py`. **Escalation condition.** A defect found in a flag that cannot be repaired within the flag's own opt-in code is recorded, the flag is left off, and the stage is reported as not accepted; the default path is never touched.

### G23 PROGRESS LOG (2026-10-12 evening; the smoke sessions `g55-smoke-63` / `-256` prove FUNCTION and token identity; the criteria above are unchanged and the timing sessions that judge them are queued as `g58-chain`)
Power state: the first probe ran on battery (D74); every session below ran on mains (each arm line of the wrapper log says `[mains ...]`; the machine was plugged in between the probe and the first session). All sessions attested by the provenance gate, 0 refused.
- **g41 (first build with the three flags; `g55-smoke-63-g41`).** B arms ran and were exact; five defects found, each in a flag's own opt-in code, each repaired and the same session rerun: (1) `--pp-stock-below` decoded the prompt with 63 output rows while the context reserves one (`GGML_ASSERT(n_outputs_max <= cparams.n_outputs_max)`): the stock branch now asks for logits at the last prompt position only; (2) the telemetry checker (the runner's own `check_telemetry`, exit 1 at the end of the run) knew nothing of CPU-leg layers: no `copy_complete` before their `layer_compute`, no deterministic slot sequence: it now reads a `pp_cpu_layers` event and skips those layers in the copy-completed rule, the copy_submit slot rule and the slot_evict rule (one `served_layer` helper); (3) a stale DUPLICATE request re-staged a layer the slot already held and that had been consumed (`copy_submit layer 35` four times in a pass): with paced copies the worker is busy for a whole bank, so the duplicate was popped late, found the slot released (`free_slot == 1`) and staged over it, at the end of the prefill into a slot buffer that `begin_tg_stock()` had just released (`CUDA error: invalid argument` in `ggml_backend_cuda_set_tensor_async`): `af_submit_copy` now returns for a layer the slot already holds (gated by pacing or a CPU leg, so the historical path is untouched), and the worker is waited for (`af_wait_idle`) before the pass ends and before `begin_tg_stock()`; (4) the host test `test-layer-streaming-cpu-leg` died with an integer divide by zero because the new `pp_cpu_layers` event called `ggml_time_us()` before `ggml_time_init()`: the event is written only when a telemetry sink exists; (5) `--pp-repeat 2` with the stock prompt path re-ran the layer-major per-pass prebuild on a released slab (`activation slab A exhausted`): skipped on that path.
- **g43 (the build the sessions use; `bin/g43`, exe `628bcb51...`, `llama.dll` `82715c73...`).** `ctest` 11/11 (C1). `g55-smoke-63` and `-256`: every arm exit 0; the 8 generated token IDs are IDENTICAL in all nine arms of both sessions (LM40 g40 control, LM41 g41 flags off, BPA, BPB, SP1, SP0, HYC, HY2, HY2P): B1, A1 and C2/C3 at these two lengths hold. Prefill wall of the LAST (warm) pass, single rep, ms: at 63 tokens LM40 1341, LM41 1347, BPA 1315, BPB 1372, SP1 697, SP0 505, HYC 394, HY2 753, HY2P 568; at 256 tokens LM40 1390, LM41 1393, BPA 1320, BPB 1373, SP1 1123, SP0 1286, HYC 1376, HY2 1291, **HY2P 930** (stock binary, g49: 1,113). These single reps are NOT the criteria's evidence (two counterbalanced reps in the sessions below); they decided what to queue.
- **B2 on the g41 trace (`g55-smoke-63-g41`, warm pass, 63 tokens).** Attention layers' `layer_compute`: LM41 33.1 ms (median), BPA 5.8 ms, BPB 4.0 ms (the stall is gone); recurrent layers 1.45 ms unpaced, 3.67 ms (BPA) and 2.9 ms (BPB): the tiny copies of a recurrent layer used to start in the 2 ms gaps between banks, and a paced bank leaves none (period 32.4 ms against 33.1 ms unpaced: pacing removed the gaps and made the all-GPU warm prefill 2-5% FASTER at 63 and 256 tokens in the single reps, not slower). `async_feed_submit` carries `pieces` = 27-32 (16 MiB) / 108-125 (4 MiB) and `pace_wait_us` ~32 ms on every worker-fed bank.

### G23 RESULTS (2026-10-12 night; the contract above, criteria unchanged; FINAL build g44 = `bin/g44`: runner exe sha256 `646a673c708a00937b3cdf27138f60fa434f6dc34466013fd8b64390ea30ee36`, `llama.dll` `63562f9c1968ca08f974ff8ceef2167f132f786bc8dee6fb2b320d587596d235`, `ggml-cuda.dll` unchanged from g40 (`0cf7d5e3...`), engine-tree digest `EA3E7D36C9E1E3F6`; the intermediate build g43 (`bin/g43`, exe `628bcb51...`) ran `g57-*`, `g50-*`, `g56-*`, `g64-*`, `g65-*` and is named where used; mains power; every cited session attested by the provenance gate, 0 refused; two counterbalanced reps unless noted; tables are medians of the reps; `python tools/g23-table.py hybrid|stock|pace|final|ident|deep`)
**What was built (nested tree, opt-in, default off; record = the regenerated patch).** (A) `--pp-stock-below N [--pp-stock-offload 0|1]`: a prompt shorter than N tokens is decoded through the stock graph path (the path the hybrid decode uses), the scheduler's op offload on or off. (B) `--pp-copy-piece-mib P --pp-copy-window W`: the feed worker issues each 144 MiB member in pieces with at most W in flight, releases the shared mutex while it does so (`slot.stage_il` + a condition variable guard the slot), and also carries the preload banks of a CPU-leg plan; the request queue is de-duplicated, unbounded and served smallest layer first in the paced / CPU-leg modes; a layer the slot already holds is not staged again; the worker is waited for before slots are released. (C) `--pp-cpu-layers L|all`: the listed layers run their routed experts on the CPU from the host banks (no slot, no copy, no consumer event, op offload off, CPU threads set to the batch count). Also `--pp-threadpool 1` (an explicit persistent CPU pool for the prefill; found useless, stays off), the telemetry events `pp_copy_pacing` / `pp_cpu_layers` and the fields `pieces` / `pace_wait_us`, the runner checker's CPU-leg rules, the host test `test-layer-streaming-cpu-leg`. About 600 process launches under the GPU lock in 56 wrapper invocations (3 of them died at launch on a wrong prompt name in my generator, before any model load, and were rerun).
