| 48 | MTP self-speculation (row 16) keeps its gain at long fills. | Built and measured at 50k / 100k / 200k with the deep recipe against the non-MTP recipe on the same prompts (`g41-qadm*` against `g41-qad*`, G19 / G20). | DISPROVEN at depth: TG 75.8 / 59.6 / 40.5 tok/s against 93.8 / 90.7 / 80.8 (-19% / -34% / -50%), prefill 250 tok/s against 2,484 / 2,017 / 1,406, cache slots 49 / 38 / 17 against 61 / 53 / 38. | MTP needs the batch-major prefill (no slab, 8-10x slower), costs 535 MiB of draft-head memory (fewer cache slots) and its two-row verify step runs the vector attention kernel, not the packed one; the acceptance gain does not pay for it once the fill grows. | DISPROVEN as a depth configuration (stays opt-in for short fills, row 16) |
| 49 | Two layer slots with the async feed, a bf16 or f32 slab and a large ubatch each speed up the prefill; a dedicated GPU should use them all (owner direction 2026-10-12). | Measured on the 4k / 50k / 200k prompts with six prefill recipes (`g45-*`, build g40, GQA8 on), then in QA sessions at six lengths (`g47-qa*`). | **Only the ubatch matters.** One slot, bf16 slab, ubatch 512 prefills 50,000 tokens at 2,431 tok/s, two slots with f32 slab at 2,428; ubatch 1024 2,868, **2048 3,050 (+26%)**, 4096 3,062, 8192 2,918. At 200,000 tokens 512 -> 2048 gives 1,368 -> 1,581 (+16%) and 4096 adds 2.5% for 2.1 GB. At 4,096 tokens 1,553 -> 1,769 (+14%). The QA sessions of the final recipe prefill 24,000 / 50,000 / 100,000 / 200,000 / 250,000 tokens at 3,472 / 3,082 / 2,378 / 1,596 / 1,363 tok/s. | The layer's weights are loaded once per prompt, so the load is off the critical path at long prompts (slots, slab type do nothing); the ubatch sets the number of per-chunk graph launches and the width of every matmul. A larger ubatch costs prefill memory (whole-run minimum free 15,555 / 13,451 / 9,243 MiB at 2048 / 4096 / 8192 on the 50k prompt). | CONFIRMED (ubatch); DISPROVEN (slots and slab type as speed levers) |
| 50 | f16 KV with the tree's tensor-core GQA kernel beats the q8 KV vector kernel enough to pay for its memory (stop-hook diagnostic). | `g48-kv16-100k`: DKFD (q8, vector kernel), DKFDG (q8, GQA8) and DKFD16 (f16) on the same 99,956-token prompt, 2 counterbalanced reps (build g40). | DISPROVEN as a recipe: **70.38 / 98.22 / 83.51 tok/s**; the f16 cache is 2,000 MiB against 1,080 MiB at 100k, so 40 cache slots against 53, and its attention front is 6.11 ms against 5.17 ms for GQA8 on q8 (9.06 ms for the vector kernel). | Half the bytes per KV element (q8) beats the tensor-core path at one token, and the saved memory buys 13 expert slots; the GQA8 kernel reads each KV element once for all 8 heads. | DISPROVEN (q8 + GQA8 stays) |
| 51 | The layer-major prefill beats the stock prefill at every prompt length. | Sweep of ten lengths (3-2,047 tokens), stock against layer-major, 2 counterbalanced reps (`g46-*`), the corrected cost model (`tools/g22-model.py`), the stock-with-offload-off control (`g49-*`). | DISPROVEN below about **760 prompt tokens** (interpolation between 511 and 1,023): 15.2x slower at 3 tokens (1,400 against 92 ms), 5.1x at 29, 2.3x at 63, 1.5x at 256 and 511; 2.4x faster at 2,047 and 6.4x at 50,000 (G21). | The layer-major prefill streams all 40 banks (16.9 GiB, 32-41 ms each over PCIe Gen4 x8) whatever the prompt: a floor of 1.4-1.9 s. The stock prefill computes the experts of a batch of fewer than 32 tokens on the CPU and copies only the USED experts (59% of a bank at 63 tokens, 92% at 256, 99% at 511) of its 37 CPU layers for bigger batches, once per 512-token ubatch: 37 x used fraction x 32 ms reproduces the measured 727 / 905 / 1,111 / 1,248 ms within 12% (`g49-*`: with `--no-op-offload` the stock prefill is pure CPU and takes 485 / 711 / 1,143 / 1,992 / 3,912 / 8,912 ms at 63 / 125 / 256 / 511 / 1,023 / 2,047 tokens against 726 / 904 / 1,113 / 1,248 / 2,314 / 4,496 ms with the offload, i.e. the CPU path is 1.50x and 1.27x FASTER than the offload at 63 and 125 tokens, equal at 256 and 1.6-2.0x slower above 511, and it is the size the micro-benchmark predicts (the remainder over 37 x the benchmark layer time is the dense part, 35-151 ms); stock with `-ub 2048` takes 1,383 / 1,572 ms at 1,023 / 2,047 tokens, i.e. one copy of the used experts per 2048-token ubatch, faster than the layer-major prefill of the final recipe (1,501 / 1,692 ms warm) up to about 2,100 tokens). | CONFIRMED (measured; a server must send short prompts to the stock path) |
| 52 | Interleave the CPU and the GPU in the prefill of a mid-size prompt (owner idea): the CPU computes the experts of some layers from the host banks while the copy engine streams the banks of the others. | Modelled (`tools/g22-model.py` v3: per-layer-type GPU times, the controller's slot protocol, the DRAM contention measured by `dram-share`, a searched assignment); the CPU leg's per-layer cost is MEASURED in the stock graph path (`g49-*`, the size the micro-benchmark predicts); the controller changes (`--pp-cpu-layers`) are written and syntax-checked, NOT built or run yet. | OPEN: the model gives +8-17% over the best all-GPU configuration at 64-2,047 tokens (base), +1-17% under pessimistic contention, and at most +7-15% over the best EXISTING path once the stock paths are counted (stock CPU path below 128 tokens, stock offload with ubatch 2048 up to 2,100); it is negative at 32 tokens and below because attention layers stall (row 56). | The link is the floor of every path that streams banks (37-40 banks x 32-41 ms); the CPU leg removes banks from the link in proportion to its share, but a CPU layer costs 35-190 ms at 256-2,048 tokens against 33-41 ms for a copy, so it only pays for the cheaper recurrent layers and only where the attention part of the layer is not stalled behind the DMA. | CONFIRMED with paced copies (rows 61, 62); the unpaced form is DISPROVEN |
| 53 | After a request, preload the first two layers so the next request starts at once (owner idea). | Bounded with the model (`tools/g22-model.py --pre`): K preloaded banks save K x 32-41 ms. | BOUNDED, not built: 82 ms for two banks, 4-6% of the 1.4-1.9 s floor; the preloaded banks must stay in VRAM through the decode, i.e. they cost decode cache slots (each bank is 6.3 slots of 68.27 MiB). | Link time is not the only price; the saving is small against the floor. | BOUNDED |
| 54 | The copy of a bank can run at the pinned rate (14.2 GB/s, 32 ms) instead of 40 ms. | Observed in the `g46-2048` trace: bank 0 copied in 31.7 ms (14.3 GB/s, nothing else on the link), later banks in 40.5-40.8 ms while the GPU computed or waited. | OPEN (not tested): in the nsys profile of the 63-token run (`g54-nsys-pp`) every bank takes 31.5 ms (three 144 MiB members of 10.5 ms, 14.4 GB/s) from the same registered mapping, so the mapping CAN run at the pinned rate; the 2,047-token traces fit 41 ms, which suggests the compute of the layers slows the DMA by about 25% at that length. | Hypothesis, not established: physical page contiguity of the registered mmap pages (the first banks were read into free memory first), or an effect of the concurrent compute. A test needs a microbenchmark of the registered spans bank by bank, and a pinned mirror costs 16.9 GiB of RAM. | OPEN |
| 55 | A host-resident activation slab removes the largest VRAM consumer of the prefill at depth (8,192-16,384 B per prompt token). | Design read from the code; not built. | OPEN: would hold the strict keep-free rule at any fill at a modelled 3-7% prefill cost (about 63 GB of activation traffic over a 14 GB/s link during a 125 s prefill at 200k). | The scheduler assigns every op that touches a tensor of the CUDA host buffer type to the CPU backend on a discrete GPU (`ggml_backend_cuda_device_supports_buft` accepts a host buffer only for integrated GPUs), so a plain host slab would move the layer's activation ops to the CPU; the slab needs explicit per-chunk staging copies on a copy stream instead. | OPEN |
| 56 | An attention layer of the layer-major prefill costs a fixed ~32 ms whatever the prompt length (3 tokens: 32.5 ms, 2,047: 38.9 ms; a recurrent layer 0.7-9.3 ms). | Per-layer compute of all ten sweep traces (`tools/g22-trace-sim.py`), then an nsys profile of the 63-token run (`g54-nsys-pp`, runtime API and memcpy tables joined by correlation id). | **ROOT-CAUSED, not a GPU cost:** the GPU kernels of the layer take microseconds. Nine times in the profiled prefill a 256 KiB host-to-device input copy of the COMPUTE stream (positions and KV indices, which only attention layers use) starts 31.5 ms after it was submitted, exactly when the 453 MB bank copy of the COPY stream that was running when it was queued ends (exec 11,928.7-11,939.3 ms, the small copy 11,939.25 ms): head-of-line blocking on the one copy engine. | The DMA engine runs commands in order and a bank is 3 commands of 144 MiB (10.5 ms each), so a small copy that arrives behind them waits up to a whole bank. It costs nothing while the prefill is copy-bound (the next layer needs the next bank anyway) and 32 ms per attention layer (10 per prompt) as soon as a layer must not wait for the DMA: the CPU leg of row 52, any run with idle copy time. Fix to test: submit the bank copy in 2-4 MiB pieces (head-of-line wait <= 0.3 ms). | ROOT-CAUSED and FIXED by paced copies (rows 58, 59) |
| 57 | More layer slots speed up the layer-major prefill of a short prompt (replay of the 2,047-token trace: 3 slots -3%, 4 -5%, 6-8 -9..-11%). | Replay with the traced per-layer compute (`tools/g22-trace-sim.py`), then measured: 2, 4, 6 and 8 slots, WARM second pass of the same process (`--pp-repeat 2`), 6 lengths x 2 reps (`g53-*`). | DISPROVEN for the warm prefill, true only for a cold start: warm times 1,336 / 1,391 / 1,429 / 1,501 / 1,692 / 2,126 ms (2 slots) against 1,334 / 1,386 / 1,418 / 1,489 / 1,650 / 2,042 ms (8 slots) at 63 / 255 / 511 / 1,023 / 2,047 / 4,096 tokens: -0.1% / -0.4% / -0.8% / -0.8% / -2.5% / -4.0%. The FIRST pass of a fresh process is 0.2-0.4 s slower than the second (1,740 against 1,336 ms at 63 tokens, 2,002 against 1,692 at 2,047), and deeper slots recover about 10% of it. | The replay's gain was the one-time 190 ms of kernel loading in the first layer (layer 0 computes in 186-200 ms against 9 ms for the layers that follow), which a deep slot window absorbs; the warm prefill is copy-bound at 33-37 ms per bank and two slots already keep the copy engine busy. The stock `prompt eval time` excludes its own warm-up (llama.cpp warms up before the timer) while the layer-major sweep of g46 included it: the crossover with stock moves from about 760 to about 600 tokens when both are warm. | CLOSED; warm numbers are the ones to quote |
| 58 | Cut a bank copy into pieces so that a small copy of the compute stream is not blocked behind it. | `tools/ce-hol-bench` (432 MiB bank, a 256 KiB copy submitted 3 ms after it starts; 16 variants, 9 trials, battery power); output `build/llama-cuda/ce-hol-1.txt`. | DISPROVEN for pieces that are all enqueued at once (the small copy still waits 30.2-31.9 ms); CONFIRMED when the pieces in flight are bounded (2 MiB x 2: 0.24 ms, 16 MiB x 2: 2.1 ms, at +2.5% to +20% of the bank time). | One copy engine runs commands in submission order; a later command on another stream sits behind everything already queued. The bound on the queue is what bounds the wait; each paced piece costs ~30 us of host round trip. | CONFIRMED (paced) |
| 59 | Paced bank copies in the feed worker (`--pp-copy-piece-mib 16 --pp-copy-window 2`, stage B). | Built (mutex released around the issue, slot guard, de-duplicated unbounded smallest-first queue, paced preload), host-tested for the lifecycle, run in `g57`, `g68`, `g70`, `g76` (63 to 200,000 tokens). | CONFIRMED: attention layers 33-36 ms -> 5.8-8.5 ms; the all-GPU layer-major prefill -2.5 / -5.4 / -10.7 / -19 / -32.6 / -10.9 / -4.5 / -1.7 / -0.5% at 63 / 256 / 1,023 / 2,047 / 4,096 / 24,000 / 50,000 / 100,000 / 200,000 tokens, tokens identical, default-off identical to g40 within +-0.8%. | The unpaced pipeline hid the stall behind the copy only while a layer's compute stayed under a bank copy; paced, the pipeline sits on the copy floor (40 x 33 ms = 1.32 s). | CONFIRMED |
| 60 | A stock-graph prompt path in the runner (`--pp-stock-below`, stage A). | Built, run in `g56` at 3-2,047 tokens against the stock binary, QA smoke in `g59`. | CONFIRMED for the op-offload arms (within +-4% of the stock binary at 63-2,047 tokens; 19 ms for 3 tokens); the CPU-only arm is faster below ~125 tokens and 30-60% slower from 511 (row 63). | Same graphs through the same `llama_decode`; the runner's warm second pass reuses a built graph. | CONFIRMED (op offload); see row 63 |
| 61 | The CPU/GPU interleave with unpaced copies (`--pp-cpu-layers`, first form, build g43). | Built; `g50-<N>` at 29-2,047 tokens, 9 arms, 2 reps. | DISPROVEN as built: 1.13-1.15x of the best stock path at 256 tokens (HY2 1,294, HY4 1,291 against STKU 1,130). | Every CPU layer's result copy and the next layer's inputs wait behind the in-flight bank (33 ms); the v3 model charged the stall to attention layers only (+19% / +24% model error). | DISPROVEN (unpaced) |
| 62 | The interleave with paced copies, paced preload and the unbounded smallest-first queue. | `g50-<N>` (g43: 0.80-0.90x at 256-511 tokens), then fix 3 and `g68-<N>` (g44). | CONFIRMED: 0.74 / 0.78 / 0.83 / 0.82 of the best stock path at 256 / 511 / 1,023 / 2,047 tokens, both reps, tokens identical, QA smoke passes; v4 model within -0.5 .. -4.2%. | The CPU takes banks off the link in proportion to its share; paced copies let its results through; the preload and the queue no longer idle the link. | CONFIRMED |
| 63 | The runner's CPU-only paths are as fast as the stock binary's. | `g56` (SP0), `g50` (HYC), then six hypotheses in `g64`, `g65`, `g66`, `g67`, `g72`, `g78`. | DISPROVEN as stated: 30-60% slower from ~511 tokens, up to 1.6x rep-to-rep spread, faster below ~125 tokens. Six candidate causes falsified (engine threads, mmap, thread count, explicit pool, environment variables, core placement). | Not established (see G23 RESULTS, what is NOT established). | CLOSED by row 65 (cause found; repair `--gpu-keepalive`) |
| 64 | A server-grade integration: choose the plan by prompt length and a paced worker inside the product. | Not built. | OPEN: the harness shows what the mechanisms buy; a product needs the plan table per length, the pacing flags as defaults and the CPU leg inside its scheduler. | n/a | OPEN |
| 65 | The PCIe link of this laptop GPU keeps its speed while the GPU idles. | `tools/pcie-state-bench` (standalone), nvidia-smi around the runs (`g81`), nsys timelines (`g80`), keep-alive repair (`g84`, `g85`, build g45). | DISPROVEN: after ~10 s of GPU utilisation under ~10% the link falls Gen4 -> Gen2 -> Gen1 (a 32 MiB copy 2.33 -> 9.15 -> 18.3 ms) and needs ~0.25 s of traffic to climb back; only a busy GPU (a spinning kernel on one SM) keeps Gen4. | Power management of the link/GPU state (mechanism inferred, not read from the driver). Explains row 63 completely: SP0 with the keep-alive is 31-32% faster and within +8% of stock. | CONFIRMED (cause of row 63) |
## DECISION LOG (continued 7, 2026-10-12; G13 .. G18)
Format: question - options - choice - reason - reversible? - evidence. All are the agent's decisions under the owner's grant.
- D46 G13-1 is ambiguous ("the top-2 predicted experts that are NOT resident"). Options: the whole ranking (P-B) or the predictor's top 12 (P-C). Choice: the stricter reading governs and G13-1 is recorded as FAILED (precision 0.338 against 0.40), P-C (0.437) reported beside it. Reason: AGENTS.md rule for ambiguous goal text. Not reversible once the numbers were read. Evidence: G13 RESULT.
- D47 After G13 the VRAM-side prefetch is not built. Options: build it (a GPU kernel for the router, a hint mailbox, intra-token map updates) for a modelled 7-9% that a 124%-loaded link would eat; or use the same predictor to warm the host's own caches. Choice: the host cache (G15), judged by an offline screen on the exact replay and a host micro-benchmark BEFORE any engine run. Reversible (opt-in env). Evidence: G13 RESULT, G15 contract.
- D48 Host leg: replace the 8-12 node ggml graph by one parallel region with three spin barriers that calls the same kernels (G14). Options: tune the graph (row 32 showed no lever), a custom ggml op, or a team of our own. Choice: our own team (`src/llama-moe-lean.h`), with the graph kept as the control and a self-check mode that serves every request twice and compares bits. Products and sums in separate loops so that no compiler contracts them. Reversible (env). Evidence: 3,000 host checks, 30,000 compared requests, G14.
- D49 The first self-check ran the lean team on the cores of the graph's spinning pool and crawled (100 ms per request). Choice: pin the self-check team to CCD1 and rerun, instead of reading the crawl as a result. Evidence: G14 RESULTS.
- D50 Prefetch implementation: software prefetch instructions or plain loads. Choice: plain loads, after `--bench-pf` showed the instructions warm about 40% of what loads warm; the contract text was amended before any engine run (the criteria did not change). Evidence: G15 contract amendment.
- D51 The first G15 sessions were UNCERTIFIED (a literal `$B` in the attested exe path). Choice: quarantine them (`build/llama-cuda/g31-uncertified/`), fix the queue script, rerun everything certified. All G15 numbers quoted are from `g32-*`. Reversible: no (the evidence is the rerun). Evidence: G15 RESULTS.
- D52 G14 and G15 are accepted on their contracts; G15-4's wording about slow requests is reported as held for the quiet soak and as a class of event (CPU 0) for the 512-token runs, not rewritten. Evidence: the two acceptance blocks.
- D53 A stall diagnostic goes into the team (time stamp counter and `rdtscp` per barrier) because the stalls were the one thing three rounds could not attribute. It is cheap (a few time stamps per request) and stays in. Evidence: G16 contract and results.
- D54 G16-1's baseline clause (at least 5 events in the old-placement arm) failed in the first session (2 events). Choice: repeat the same validation with 2,048-token runs (more events per GPU minute) instead of dropping or relaxing the clause; it held (12 events, all CPU 0). The decision on the defaults rests on both sessions. Evidence: G16 RESULTS.
- D55 The team's CPU layout and the decoding thread's pin become DEFAULTS of the lean team (machine-specific: core 0 is this machine's interrupt core), overridable by env, with the graph leg untouched as the control. Reversible. Evidence: G16 RESULTS, `g35-def` (+3.58%).
- D56 Policy variants: the combinations were scored after the single variants had failed the bar (post-hoc, and said so); to make that safe the engine A/B was pre-registered with a held-out prompt and a candidate chosen by a rule fixed before the run. The candidate failed the held-out prompt by 3.55%. Rows 17, 22 and 25 are CLOSED, not accepted. Evidence: G17 / G17c.
- D57 The final comparison (G18) uses the owner's keep-free definition (69 slots), not the poller-unit 8,192 MiB cap of the G14-G17 sessions (81 slots), and drops the per-layer split arm. Evidence: G18 contract and results.
- D58 Row 34's pipelined launch (hiding about 0.7 ms of host chain per token, estimated +9..11%) is NOT implemented. Reason: it needs a double-buffered routing trace and publish state, graph instances per parity, a device-resident token feed and an event-based `on_token`, i.e. a rewrite of the hybrid's per-token protocol, for a gain that the sampling probe (+1.7% at most for the argmax half) does not support on its own. It stays OPEN with its requirements recorded; reopen if the owner wants the last 10%.
- D59 Row 23 (GPU-served misses) is closed as BOUNDED without building the second miss path: the missing term was measured (G17c: 1.69 MiB of extra PCIe traffic costs the token about 24 us, a PCIe-served miss saves the CPU path 36 us), which cancels most of the gain. Reversible: reopen with a different link (Gen5 x16) or a copy-engine priority scheme.
- D60 Commits: local, outer repo only (docs, tools, specs); `AGENTS.md` and the `third_party/llama.cpp` gitlink stay unstaged; the nested tree stays uncommitted and its record is the regenerated patch. No push, no pull request (never autonomous).
- D61 Doc-size debt. Question: the size guard of `tools/run-tests.ps1 -Suite host` FAILS on `docs/plans/generic-prefill-layer-residency-plan.md` (485,036 B, 101.0% of the 480,000 B band; last touched before this goal, in `a5fa223`) and halts the suite BEFORE its ctest step. Options: (1) the repo's own `tools/resplit-gplr-plan.py --apply`; (2) weaken or bypass the guard; (3) leave the file and run the identical ctest command directly. Choice: (1) was tried on 2026-10-12 and REVERTED: the splitter classifies all 26 remaining blocks as archivable and moves all of them (live file 7,795 B with no block heading, `--verify` CANNOT-EVALUATE, archive 834 KB = 174% of the band, i.e. it would fail the same guard); `git checkout` of the three files restored the committed state (they held no change of mine, `--verify` PASS again). (2) is a moved bar and is not allowed. (3) chosen: the ctest command the suite prints was run directly on the freshly rebuilt tree (all targets built; the only link error is the upstream `test-unicode`, a DLL-export problem unrelated to this tree): 78/78 passed, plus `test-moe-lean-expert` (3,001 checks, 0 failures), `test-layer-streaming-route-topk`, `test-layer-streaming-host-mem` (3/3). A real fix needs a multi-shard archive in the splitter, i.e. a change to another workstream's tool and document, outside this goal. Reversible: yes. The guard protects `ctx_search` visibility only, and the owner forbids the ctx tools (memory).
- D62 Cost and portability. Accept 17.5 GiB of pinned RAM and 8 busy-waiting threads as the price of the Goal Charter's order (speed first, VRAM second); the team placement stays an environment-overridable default of this machine (`LLAMA_MOE_HYBRID_LEAN_CPUS`, `_MAIN_CPU`, `_SPIN_US`); NO topology probe is built, because it could not be validated on a second machine and on this one it would have to reproduce the existing default. Reversible. Evidence: G16 RESULTS, row 28.
- D63 Deep-fill validation (G19) is run. Reason: every speed number so far comes from fills of at most 24,000 tokens while the target is a 262,144-token window; this is the largest honest gap in the claim. Contract written before the first run. Evidence: G19 CONTRACT.

## DECISION LOG (continued 8, 2026-10-12; G19 .. G22)
Format: question - options - choice - reason - reversible? - evidence. All are the agent's decisions under the owner's grant. D63 (G19 is run) and D64 (build g36) are recorded above.
- D65 Reading of the owner's keep-free rule (`--fit-target 16000`: at least 16,000 MiB free in the CUDA runtime's view) once the owner said the GPU is dedicated. Options: keep the whole-run reading; read it in the decode phase (after the prefill's slots, slab and scheduler buffers are released and the cache is resized). Choice: both are measured and reported side by side in every deep-fill row; the decode-phase reading is the one the G21 contract is judged on, G19-2 keeps its whole-run wording and its recorded miss, and the plain batch-major recipe is the configuration that meets the whole-run reading at 200k and 250k. Reason: with nothing else on the card the prefill may use it, and the fast recipe's whole-run minimum is 15,555 MiB already at 50k. Reversible: yes (the numbers for both readings are in the tables). Evidence: G21 contract and results, G19 FINAL STATUS, ledger row 46.
- D66 Process incident and the digest. A patch script for the G22 stage C edits was run twice by mistake while `g47-fin100000` ran (about 4 s and 80 s in the nested tree), after a "dry-run" switch that never existed was added to its helper through a heredoc that mangled the backslashes. Choice: revert with the exact reverse edits, restore the files' modification times to the recorded ones, verify the digest, disclose in the G21 results, keep every nested edit script renamed `.txt` while a session is queued, give each a dry-run first exercised on a throwaway file, and run it first against a COPY of the files. The session's pre and post digests agree; the digest is a hash of git's diff text and depends on the console code page that decodes it (two values for the same tree, three seconds apart). Reversible: not needed. Evidence: G21 results (process notes), AGENTS.md gotchas, the `heredoc-backslashes` memory.
- D67 Reading of G19 after the repairs. G19-1 and G19-3 are met; G19-2 is met over the whole run by no LAYER-MAJOR recipe at 200k (333 MiB short for the best one) and by the batch-major recipe; no criterion is rewritten to fit. The owner can reopen it: the open fix for the fast recipe is ledger row 55 (activation slab off the card), not built.
- D68 MTP review items NOT fixed. A read-only review of the MTP path of the runner listed latent defects that no cited session can reach: a corner of the prebuild under `--mtp`, `--tg-first-pp-token` being ignored under `--mtp`, `pending_h` starting at zero, missing n_ctx and ubatch checks. The padding defect (row 45), which a cited session DID hit, is fixed. Reason: MTP is not in the recommended configuration at depth (row 48) and no cited number depends on the others. Reversible: yes.
- D69 G22 scope. The owner's ideas are answered by measurement and a model first (G22 STUDY); prototypes follow as stages with their own contracts written before their first run: A (the runner runs short prompts through the stock graph path with the op offload set by length; this realises "CPU path below ~200 tokens, offload path to ~2,100" inside the final configuration), B (bank copies in 2-4 MiB pieces: the test of the attention-layer stall), C (`--pp-cpu-layers`, the CPU leg of the layer-major controller). Reason: the model says the interleave pays +1-15% over the best existing path and loses below 128 tokens until stage B removes the stall, so B comes first. The all-CPU plan is NOT run through the layer-major controller for short prompts (its attention layers stall: 383 ms at 3 tokens against 82 ms for the stock path).
- D70 Comparisons of the layer-major prefill with stock use the WARM pass from now on (`--pp-repeat 2`, last pass). The cold numbers of `g46-*` stay as measured and are labelled: llama.cpp warms up before its timer, a fresh layer-major process does not (about 0.2-0.4 s). Stock with `-ub 2048` (STKU) is reported as a comparator; the headline comparisons stay against the owner's flags.
- D71 Order of work. Docs, tools and specs are committed locally (outer repo only; `AGENTS.md` and the `third_party/llama.cpp` gitlink not staged; no push) BEFORE any new nested edit; the nested tree stays uncommitted and its record is the regenerated patch. Evidence: D60.

- D72 Order and build of the G22 prototype. Options: one stage per build; one build with all three opt-in edits. Choice: ONE build (g41) with all three, because each edit is default-off and the identity criteria (B4/A4) compare g41 without flags against g40 in the same session; three rebuilds of `llama.dll` (about 10 minutes each at 12 jobs) would cost more than they protect. Reversible: yes (the nested patch records the tree; the exact edit scripts are `stage-c/apply_g22_cpuleg.py.txt`, `g23_pacing_apply.py.txt`, `g23_stockpath_apply.py.txt`). Evidence: this section.
- D73 Pacing design, from the G23 PROBE. Options: unpaced pieces (disproven by the probe: the small copy still waits for the whole bank), a gate that holds the next bank until the layer's small copies are issued (the same bubble as pacing, and it needs the compute thread to signal), pre-uploading the layer-invariant inputs once per chunk (removes the attention-layer stall only, not the result copy of a CPU layer), an SM-driven copy through mapped host memory (steals SMs from the compute that the long-prompt prefill is bound by), paced pieces with a bounded number in flight. Choice: paced pieces, default 16 MiB x 2 (2.1 ms, +2.5% of the copy rate), opt-in; the feed worker releases the shared mutex while it issues a bank and the slot carries `stage_il` so the main thread's `load_layer` / `wait_slot_ready` wait on a condition variable for it; the main thread's preload stays unpaced; the request queue is de-duplicated and 2 deep while pacing or a CPU leg is on. Reversible: yes. Evidence: G23 PROBE.
- D74 Power. The machine reports `ac_line: battery` at 2026-10-12 19:07 (battery 100%, GPU power cap Active). The owner's own record says the same flags run 2.2x slower on battery (AGENTS.md, g19-fs). Choice: no timing session on battery; function and exactness runs (A1, A3, B1, B2, C1-C3) are allowed and are labelled "battery"; every timing criterion waits for mains; the power state is read with `tools/run-tg-arms.py power_state()` before each session. Reversible: n/a.
- D75 The all-GPU prefill is copy-bound, so pacing is a CPU-leg tool. In the warm pass at 63 tokens banks complete every 33.0 ms with the attention layers' 33 ms compute hidden (trace `g53-63-LM2W_r1`), 1.2 ms per bank above the probe's pure rate; pacing therefore can only cost the all-GPU prefill (B3 bounds that cost) and is enabled for the hybrid arms. The 1.2 ms per bank (4%) between the probe and the run is unexplained (registered mmap pages instead of one pinned allocation is the first candidate: scattered 4 KiB pages need more DMA descriptors); it is not pursued here.

- D76 Widened comparison sets, recorded BEFORE the timing sessions of G23 ran (the smoke sessions had shown HYC at 63 tokens and HY2P at 256 tokens, single reps). C4's "interleave" arms are every `--pp-cpu-layers` arm of the session {HYC, HY2, HY2P, HY4, HY4P}, its reference arms {STK, STKNO, STKU, LM2W (warm)}; the 0.97 bar and the both-reps rule are unchanged. A2 compares each runner stock-path arm's WARM (last) pass with the matching stock binary arm's prompt-eval wall in the same session. The chain order is B (g57), C (g50), A (g56), because C is the open question.

- D77 Recommended opt-in additions to the final configuration (no default changes): `--pp-copy-piece-mib 16 --pp-copy-window 2` for every prompt length (it never cost time in the 63 to 200,000 token range tested: -0.5% at 200k to -32.6% at 4,096); for prompts up to 125 tokens `--pp-cpu-layers all`; for 256 to 2,047 tokens a paced hybrid plan (the lists in G23 RESULTS; 4 slots up to 511 tokens, 8 slots from 1,023). `--pp-stock-below` stays an instrument (the controller's CPU path is faster where the stock path would be chosen, and the op-offload stock arms are equal to the stock binary). Reversible: yes (flags).
- D78 Reading of B2's recurrent clause (strict): 16 x 2 at 63 tokens is +2.09 ms against a +-2 band, so the clause is MISSED by 0.09 ms there and is met by 4 x 3 everywhere. Not repaired and not moved: the added latency is the mechanism (a small copy now waits for W pieces instead of for a bank), the wall time improves (B3), and a configuration that meets the clause exists. 16 x 2 stays the recommended default because it is the faster one at 63-1,023 tokens (-2.8 .. -11.9% against +1.7 .. -7.9%).
- D79 Reading of A2: a deviation caused by an outlier rep of the stock side is not a runner deviation (STK 1,122 / 1,470, listed); a runner FASTER than the stock binary at small N is explained by the reused graph of the second pass; the CPU-only arm's slowness from 511 tokens is NOT explained and is recorded as not met (row 63). No claim depends on it.
- D80 The CPU-only anomaly is not pursued further: six causes are falsified, the interleaved plans (the claim) do not depend on it, and the remaining candidates (memory placement of the mapped pages, barrier imbalance in the OpenMP regions) need a profiler this environment does not have. It stays OPEN in the ledger.
- D81 Which build supports which claim: `g44` for Tables 1 and 2 (rows 100,000 and 200,000 tokens included), identity, QA, decode guard; `g43` for Table 3 and for `g57` (B2 / B3 numbers at 63-1,023 tokens); `g50` (g43) is the unpaced / pre-fix state and is quoted only as history (row 61). The four defects found after g41 are listed in the progress log and in G23 RESULTS.
- D82 `validate_telemetry` is left unchanged (it is the JSON validator of older gates; `check_telemetry`, the runner's own exit status, is CPU-leg aware). `--pp-threadpool` stays in the code, off, as a negative result with its test (`g66`).
- D83 Process: three sessions (`g56-stock-10 / -14 / -29`) died at launch because the generator keyed the sweep prompt on the token count instead of the file number (`sweep-8` holds 10 tokens, `-16` 14, `-32` 29); they were fixed and rerun on the same build g43. A decode regression guard (`g73`, `g73b`) was added because the new code touches the worker that the decode transition waits for.

- D84 `--gpu-keepalive` stays opt-in and OFF: it repairs the CPU-only paths (-31 / -32%) and does nothing for the cold first pass beyond a 100 ms gain at 63 tokens, while it slows the hybrid warm pass by 5.6-21% (probably the shared power budget); it stops before the generation. Not part of the recommended recipe (D77 unchanged). Reversible: yes.
- D85 Ledger row 63 is closed by repair (SP0K -31 / -32%, within +8% of stock = the 40-against-37 CPU layers), row 65 records the mechanism. The A2 and C-criteria statuses of G23 are NOT changed: the measurements stand as taken (the keep-alive is a repair of the instrument, not a re-reading of the old numbers).
- D86 Environment fact for every later timing: do not start a transfer-bound timed region after a GPU-idle phase of ~12 s or more without a warm-up burst of about 0.3 s of traffic (or keep the GPU busy); CPU-bound phases longer than ~10 s with an idle GPU make every GPU transfer in them up to 8x slower. Recorded in `docs/AGENT_ENVIRONMENT.md` and AGENTS.md.

## OWNER SUMMARY (AGENTS.md five-part form; autonomous decisions, NOT owner approval) - ADDENDUM 2026-10-12 night after G23 (the G22 prototype: stock path, paced copies, CPU/GPU interleave; build g44)
**1. Original task.** The owner asked (chat, 2026-10-12): from which prompt length does the layer-major prefill beat the stock prefill, and can the CPU and the GPU share a short prefill (CPU layers while the copy engine loads other layers; preload at idle; hide the copy time)? The study (G22) found the layer-major prefill has a ~1.3 s floor (40 expert banks over PCIe) and loses below ~600 tokens, and that an attention layer of the prefill stalls 33 ms behind the bank copy. G23 built and measured the three prototype stages the study ended with.
**2. How it works, in plain words.** The prefill walks the 40 layers; the 432 MiB expert bank of each layer must be copied to the GPU, one copy at a time. (B) A small copy that the GPU needs (a layer's inputs, a CPU layer's result) used to wait behind the whole bank in flight; issuing the bank in 16 MiB pieces with two in flight lets it through after ~2 ms, at 2.5% of the copy speed. (C) With that fixed, the CPU can take some layers' experts itself while the copy engine streams the others: fewer banks cross the link. For prompts up to ~125 tokens the CPU takes all of them (a CPU layer costs 8-16 ms, a bank 33 ms). (A) A short prompt can also go through the stock graph path.
**3. Glossary.** Prefill / PP: processing the prompt. TG: generating tokens. Bank: the routed-expert weights of one layer (432 MiB). Copy engine: the one DMA unit that moves banks over PCIe, running commands in order. Paced copies: bank issued in pieces, at most W in flight. CPU leg: a layer whose experts run on the CPU. STK / STKNO / STKU: the owner's stock command / without op offload / with ubatch 2048. LM2W / LM2P: the all-GPU layer-major prefill without / with paced copies. HYC / HY4P / HY8Q: all layers on the CPU / hybrid plans with 4 / 8 slots. Slot: a GPU buffer holding one bank. Warm pass: the second in-process prefill. Ratio: new / best stock, lower is better.
**4. Pros and cons (measured unless marked).** Pros: prefill wall 0.72-0.89x of the best stock path at 29-125 tokens, 0.74-0.83x at 256-2,047 (Table 1, `g68-*`, certified, 2 reps, tokens identical, QA passes `g59`); the paced all-GPU prefill is 2.5-32.6% faster than the old one from 63 to 50,000 tokens and 0.5-1.7% at 100-200k (Table 2); default behaviour unchanged (`g69`, `g73b`); the v4 model predicts the plans within ~4% (`tools/g23-model.py`). Cons: three new opt-in flags and a more intricate worker (mutex released while it issues, slot guard); the plans come from a model fitted on this machine (expected: other CPUs differ); the CPU-only paths of the runner are 15-60% slower than stock's from 500 tokens, cause unknown (unproven); a harness, not a server (unproven in a product); the gain over stock at 1,023-2,047 tokens is mostly pacing itself.
**5. Recommendation.** Keep the paced copies (adopt `--pp-copy-piece-mib 16 --pp-copy-window 2` in the final recipe: never slower in the range tested) and keep the interleave experimental until a product integrates a length policy; the owner decides whether a server integration (row 64) is worth building. Validate on mains power only, and on the owner's own CPU before trusting the plan table.
**Decision log of G23.** D72-D83 (this plan). **Acceptance contract and evidence.** "G23 CONTRACT" and "G23 RESULTS" above: B1-B4, A1-A4, C1-C5 with their numbers; B2's recurrent clause missed by 0.09 ms (D78) and A2 not met for the CPU-only arm (D79) are the two criteria not met as written.

## OWNER SUMMARY (AGENTS.md five-part form; autonomous decisions, NOT owner approval) - REWRITTEN 2026-10-12 evening after G19-G22 (final build g40, engine llama.dll `9E9B0F4B91EDF037`; certified sessions `g47-*`, `g48-*`, `g49-*`, `g53-*`, `g54-nsys-pp`)
1. **Original task / goal / idea explored.** Beat the token-generation speed of `docs/llamacpp 8gb vram 262k base.md` (30.68 tok/s, `llama-server`, 8 GB, 262,144-token window) with caching and scheduling that raise GPU utilisation, prove every idea or disprove it with its root cause. The earlier rounds built and tuned the hybrid decode engine (3.35x stock on short prompts, G18). This round asked four things. **G19:** does it still work, and stay fast, when the window is FILLED (50,000-250,000 prompt tokens)? **G20:** the one cost that grows with the fill, the decode attention: can it be made cheaper? **G21:** after the owner's two directions (use two layer slots for the prefill; the GPU is dedicated), what is the best prefill recipe and does the final configuration still win? **G22:** the owner's question about short prompts: from which length does the layer-major prefill beat the stock one, and can the CPU and the GPU share the work?
2. **How it works, in simple terms.** The model has 40 layers; each has 256 small expert networks (a "bank" is one layer's 256 experts, 432 MiB) and a router that picks 8 per token. **Decoding:** the GPU keeps the recently used experts of every layer; the few it misses are computed by 8 CPU threads from system RAM (unchanged since G18). **The attention cost that grows with the fill:** 10 of the 40 layers read the whole prompt's memory (the KV cache) for every new token; the old kernel read each KV element 8 times, once per query head, and moved only 204 GB/s; the new one (`LLAMA_FATTN_GQA8=1`) serves all 8 heads that share a KV head from one read, 3x faster at 200k tokens. **The prefill (layer-major):** instead of re-copying experts for every 512 tokens of the prompt (what stock does), load one layer's bank into the GPU once, run ALL prompt tokens through that layer, then the next; the next bank is copied while the current layer computes. The price is a floor: 40 banks over the PCIe link is 1.3 s whatever the prompt length. **On a dedicated GPU** the best prefill recipe is simply a bigger ubatch (2048 tokens per step); more slots or a bigger slab change nothing. **Short prompts:** stock copies only the experts a batch uses (59% of a bank at 63 tokens), or computes them on the CPU below 32 tokens, so it beats the layer-major prefill below about 600 tokens; with its ubatch raised to 2048 stock keeps up to about 2,100 tokens. **The profile of the layer-major prefill** found why an attention layer always costs ~32 ms: its small input copy queues behind the bank copy on the one copy engine.
3. **Glossary.** TG = token generation (decode); PP = prompt processing (prefill); tok/s = tokens per second; ubatch = tokens per step of the prefill; KV cache = the stored keys and values of the prompt that attention reads; attention layer = one of the 10 layers (every fourth) that read the KV cache; recurrent layer = one of the 30 others; bank = the 256 routed experts of one layer; slot = a place on the card where one bank is held during the prefill; slab = the activation buffer of the whole prompt kept on the card by the layer-major prefill (8 or 16 KiB per token); layer-major = for each layer, all prompt tokens; batch-major = for each batch of tokens, all layers (stock's order); STK = stock `llama-completion` with the owner's flags; STKNO = the same with `--no-op-offload` (pure CPU experts); STKU = the same with `-ub 2048`; QKFE / DKFE = the final configuration (QA run / speed run); QKFBG = the plain batch-major recipe; keep-free rule = at least 16,000 MiB of device memory stay free (the owner's `--fit-target 16000`), read over the WHOLE run or in the DECODE phase only (D65); op offload = the scheduler copying a batch's experts to the GPU instead of computing them on the CPU; warm / cold = a second / the first pass of a process; crossover = the prompt length above which one engine wins; CPU leg = running a layer's experts on the CPU while other banks stream; head-of-line blocking = a small copy waiting behind a big one in the same queue; needle QA = the programmatic answer-stability test (planted codes must appear, a negative control must fail); certified = the provenance gate attested the exact binary and sources of the session; GQA8 = the new attention kernel (8 query heads per KV head); MTP = the model's own draft head (multi-token prediction).
4. **Pros and cons** (M = measured, E = expected, U = unproven; evidence in the named plan sections and `build/llama-cuda/<session>/`).
   - M: **final configuration against stock, same sessions, certified (G21, `g47-fin*`): TG 110.0 / 97.3 / 77.9 tok/s against 33.5 / 31.0 / 25.9 at 49,956 / 99,956 / 199,956 prompt tokens = 3.28x / 3.14x / 3.01x stock (3.59x / 3.17x / 2.54x the document's 30.68); prefill 3,040 / 2,381 / 1,580 tok/s against 473 / 462 / 437 = 6.4x / 5.2x / 3.6x.** The needle QA passes with the negative control failing as designed at 4,096 / 24,000 / 50,000 / 100,000 / 200,000 / 250,000 tokens (TG 65.0-104.9 tok/s).
   - M: the GQA8 attention kernel is +24.0% / +39.1% / +64.4% TG at 49,956 / 99,956 / 199,956 tokens and changes nothing below 2,048 keys (G20; `test-backend-ops` 3/3 against the CPU reference). f16 KV with the tree's tensor-core kernel is worse (83.5 against 98.2 tok/s at 100k, 40 against 53 cache slots, `g48-kv16-100k`).
   - M: **the keep-free rule.** Decode phase: met at every fill (16,161-16,197 MiB). Whole run: NOT met by the fast recipe from 50k on (15,555 / 14,253 / 11,651 / 10,349 MiB at 50k / 100k / 200k / 250k) and by the older deep layer-major recipe at 200k (333 MiB short); MET at 200k and 250k by the plain batch-major recipe (16,161 / 16,173 MiB, QA PASS, TG 74.1 / 64.2 tok/s) at a prefill of 257 tok/s, 0.59x the stock rate. The cost of the fast recipe is the card it uses during the prefill; the fix (activation slab off the card) is designed, not built (ledger row 55).
   - M: **a real defect found and fixed:** the MTP runner padded prompts with end-of-text tokens and lost the needle at 100k (ledger row 45, root cause shown by two controlled runs); MTP re-measured on the fixed runner: +10.6% on code (137.36 against 124.19 tok/s, 4 of 4 pairs) and +1.85% on prose at a short fill, but it loses at depth (TG -19% / -34% / -50% at 50k / 100k / 200k, prefill 6-10x slower, row 48).
   - M: **short prompts (G22).** The layer-major prefill costs 1.3-1.9 s whatever the prompt (40 banks over PCIe at 31.5 ms each: the profile `g54-nsys-pp`); stock is faster up to about 600 tokens warm (760 cold), 17x faster at 3 tokens (82 against 1,400 ms cold); stock with the offload off (pure CPU) is 1.5x faster than stock at 63 tokens; stock with `-ub 2048` is faster than the layer-major prefill up to about 2,100 tokens (1,383 / 1,572 ms at 1,023 / 2,047 tokens against 1,501 / 1,692 warm). The stock prefill is a PCIe copy of the USED experts per 512-token ubatch (arithmetic within 12%, direct control with the offload off). More slots do not speed up a warm layer-major prefill (-0.1% to -4%).
   - M/E: **the CPU/GPU interleave (owner idea).** The CPU path itself is measured and matches the micro-benchmark in size; a model with the measured per-layer times says the interleave pays +1-15% over the best EXISTING path at 256-2,047 tokens and loses below 128 tokens because every attention layer waits for the in-flight bank (root cause found in the profile). It is NOT built; the controller change and the test of the stall's fix are the next stages (U).
   - M: costs and limits as before: about 17.5 GiB of pinned system RAM and 8 busy-waiting threads; machine-specific placement defaults (core 0 is this CPU's interrupt core); the GQA8 kernel needs head_dim 256, q8_0 KV, 8 query heads per KV head, one token, KV length a multiple of 256 and at least 2,048; "8 GB" is the owner's definition measured in the CUDA runtime's view, not a physical 8 GiB card (U).
   - M: process notes. 83 GPU sessions since the G18 summary (all through `tools/with-gpu-lock.ps1`, none with `-AllowUnprovenanced`); every one cited has `PROVENANCE GATE ... 0 refused`; no criterion was moved after a result; the misses are reported (G19-2 whole run at 200k for layer-major; the contract text of G21 reached the file after its QA sessions ran); one process incident (a nested-tree edit script run by mistake mid-session, reverted and verified, D66).
5. **Recommendation (autonomous acceptance, not owner approval).** **Adopt** the G21 recipe as the configuration for this machine and a 262,144-token window: `llama-layer-streaming --pp-schedule layer-major --ubatch 2048 --layer-slots 2 --act-slab-dtype f32 --residency moe-expert-bank --kv-cache q8 --allow-full-residency --no-handoff --tg-hybrid --ctx-size 262144 --tg-first-pp-token --kv-graph-pad 256 --tg-hybrid-slots auto --tg-release-compute --tg-fast-sample --kv-lazy --tg-vram-cap-mib 0 --tg-vram-keep-free-mib 16000` with `LLAMA_FATTN_GQA8=1 LLAMA_MOE_HYBRID=14 LLAMA_MOE_HYBRID_B=16 LLAMA_MOE_HYBRID_FREQ=<aer-freq-train.txt> LLAMA_CUDA_INPUT_BATCH=1 LLAMA_MOE_HYBRID_SCAN=2 LLAMA_MOE_HYBRID_LEAN=1 LLAMA_MOE_HYBRID_LEAN_PF=2` (exact command lines in the arm records of `g47-fin*`); use the plain batch-major recipe instead (the `QKFBG` arm of `docs/plans/tg-beat-stock-specs/mk_g48.py`: batch-major prefill, no MTP, a 2-slot initial cache) when the whole-run keep-free rule must hold at 200k+ and a 6x slower prefill is acceptable; send prompts shorter than about 600 tokens through the stock path (or the CPU path below ~200) rather than the layer-major prefill; leave MTP for short fills. **Keep experimenting** on three things, in this order: (1) the stock path for short prompts inside the runner (stage A of the G22 prototype), (2) bank copies in pieces to remove the attention-layer stall (stage B), then the CPU leg of the controller (stage C, modelled +1-15% over the best existing path in the 256-2,047 band), and the activation slab off the card (row 55) for the whole-run rule. **Decide / validate next:** (a) whether the decode-phase reading of the keep-free rule (D65) is what you meant; (b) whether the interleave is worth building for its small modelled gain; (c) the nested clone's commit policy (its `AGENTS.md` demands per-commit human approval; the work there stays uncommitted and is recorded in the regenerated patch); (d) a run on a physical 8 GiB card before anyone quotes "fits 8 GiB" as a hardware fact; (e) pushes and upstream material (none needed). Decision log: D1-D71 in this file; contracts G1-G22 and their results above (G19: criteria 1 and 3 met, 2 met over the whole run only by the batch-major recipe; G20: four of four; G21: three of three; G22: study, prototypes pending); acceptance scope: each acceptance certifies only what its contract defines.

## OWNER SUMMARY (AGENTS.md five-part form; autonomous decisions, NOT owner approval) - 2026-10-12 after G8-G18, kept for its numbers of the short-fill configuration (final build g35 = engine g34; certified sessions `g37-*`, `g38-*`)
1. **Original task / goal / idea explored.** Beat the token-generation speed of `docs/llamacpp 8gb vram 262k base.md` (30.68 tok/s, `llama-server`, 8 GB VRAM, 262,144-token window) with caching and scheduling that raise GPU utilisation, prove every idea or disprove it with its root cause, and never dismiss a good idea because of a bad implementation. The first rounds built a per-layer VRAM expert cache with the CPU computing the misses (2.7-2.9x stock). This round answered three audits that asked for every open idea to be built and measured: make the CPU side of that engine cheaper and smarter (a lean one-pass host leg, a predictive cache prefetch), find and fix the unexplained stalls, put MTP self-speculation on it, test the cache-policy ideas against the engine's own policy by exact replay and in the engine, and measure "8 GB" the way the owner's own command defines it (16,000 MiB kept free).
2. **How it works, in simple terms.** The model has 40 layers; each holds 256 small expert networks and a router that picks 8 of them for every token. Stock runs 37 layers' experts on the CPU. The hybrid keeps the recently used experts of every layer in GPU memory; for every token and layer the GPU runs the cached ones and posts the missing ones to a shared mailbox; 8 CPU threads read those experts from RAM, compute them and write the result back. This round: (a) the CPU threads now do a request in ONE synchronized pass (quantize the input, up and gate rows, activation, down rows and the weighted sum) instead of a graph of 8-12 steps with a barrier after each; the arithmetic is the same kernels, so the answer is bit-identical, and 20 microseconds of fixed cost per request are gone; (b) while the CPU waits for the GPU's next request (the GPU needs ~90 us for the next layer's attention) it guesses which experts that layer will miss, by running the next layer's own router on the current layer's input, and reads two of them into its own caches, so that about 20 of the ~57 cold reads per token become cache hits; (c) the CPU thread that sat on logical CPU 0, the core where this machine takes its interrupts, stalled the whole team for milliseconds (sometimes 100 ms); the threads now avoid that core and the decoding thread is pinned away from it; (d) the model's own MTP head proposes the next token and the main model checks both in one pass, which shares the GPU's weight reads (+9% on predictable output); (e) "8 GB" is applied as the owner's command applies it: the cache is sized so that at least 16,000 MiB stay free in the CUDA runtime's view, and the runner measures that every 20 ms.
3. **Glossary.** TG = token generation (decode, one token per step); PP = prompt processing (prefill); tok/s = tokens per second. MoE = mixture of experts; an expert is one feed-forward block, 256 per layer; the router picks 8. VRAM = GPU memory; slot = one expert's place in the GPU cache (the cache has N slots per layer); hit = the selected expert is in the cache, miss = it is not and the CPU computes it; hit rate = hits over selections. Host leg = the CPU side that computes the misses; graph leg = the old host leg (a ggml graph per request); lean team = the new one (8 spinning threads, three barriers, `src/llama-moe-lean.h`); request = one layer's misses sent through the mailbox. Prefetch = reading data into a cache before it is needed. Promotion = copying an expert from RAM into a GPU slot between tokens (budget B = 16 per token); EMA = the running score that ranks experts. Interrupt core = the CPU core where the OS handles device interrupts (here logical CPUs 0 and 1). MTP = multi-token prediction: the model's own draft head; acceptance = the share of drafts the main model confirms. Keep-free = the rule "at least 16,000 MiB of device memory stay free" (the owner's `--fit-target 16000`). STK = stock `llama-completion` with the owner's flags; KFB = keep-free hybrid with the graph host leg (state before this round); KFP = the final configuration; KFM = KFP plus MTP. Needle QA = a programmatic answer-stability test (planted codes must appear in the answer, a negative control must fail). Certified / UNCERTIFIED = whether the provenance gate could attest the exact binary and source tree of a session. Poller = the 50 ms `nvidia-smi` loop whose maximum is every "whole-GPU peak" quoted. The idea ledger rows 1-44 of this file define every other term at first use.
4. **Pros and cons** (M = measured, E = expected, U = unproven; evidence in the named plan sections and `build/llama-cuda/<session>/`).
   - M: **final configuration against stock, same session, owner's keep-free definition (G18; `g37-*`, 4 reps per arm):** code 124.47 tok/s (KFP) and 135.63 (KFM) against stock 37.11, i.e. **3.35x and 3.65x stock, 4.06x and 4.42x the document's 30.68**; prose 127.56 (KFP) and 129.22 (KFM) against 37.35, **3.42x and 3.46x**. Against the iso-VRAM `llama-server --fit-target 16000` (33.8 / 33.0 tok/s) the final configuration is 3.7-3.9x. In all 24 hybrid runs at least 16,191 MiB (16,161 with MTP) stayed free.
   - M: the gain of this round on the same engine, certified, code prompt: graph leg 111.57 -> lean leg 119.64 (+7.2%, G14) -> + prefetch 124.42 (+3.6%, G15) -> + placement 129.36 (+3.6%, `g35-def`), i.e. +16% at 81 slots; at the 69 slots of the owner's definition 106.02 -> 124.47 (+17.4%). MTP adds +9.0% on code, +1.3% on prose.
   - M: exactness. The lean host leg equals the graph leg bit for bit on 30,000 real requests (self-check sessions, code, prose, MTP) and on 3,000 synthetic checks over 12 quantization types; the prefetch changes no output bit (13,046 compared requests, 28 identical texts); the needle QA passes at 4,096 and 24,000 prompt tokens for both KFP and KFM, negative control failing as designed (G18). The scan, sampler, MTP and policy options change which experts are cached or the batch shape, so text differs only at near-ties (answer stability, never token identity).
   - M: the stalls are explained. All 39 requests over 3 ms in the certified G15 runs waited for the worker pinned to logical CPU 0; the OS takes its interrupts there (CPU 1: 14% interrupt + 4% DPC time). With the new placement: 0 such requests in 12 runs of 2,048 tokens against 12 and 11 in the old placement arms (G16). The old placement also hid a second effect (+230 us of gap) that the pin removes.
   - M: closed with causes. Pre-gating as a VRAM prefetch is link-bound (2 per layer = 124% of a token's link time); software prefetch instructions are dropped by the core (plain loads work); a per-layer slot split fails on a held-out prompt (-3.55%) and a larger promotion budget fails because each extra promotion costs about 24 us of GPU/PCIe time (G17c); GPU-served misses are cancelled by the same PCIe cost (row 23); MTP with two drafts does not pay; the ballast cannot emulate a smaller card under WDDM (G8).
   - M: costs. About 17.5 GiB of pinned system RAM plus 21 MiB of router copies; 8 busy-waiting high-priority threads on cores 1-8 and one pinned decoding thread (a machine-specific placement: core 0 is the interrupt core of THIS CPU, other machines need their own list via `LLAMA_MOE_HYBRID_LEAN_CPUS` / `_MAIN_CPU`); with every logical CPU busy the speed collapses (row 28); MTP needs batch-major prefill and 535 MiB more VRAM (9 fewer slots).
   - E/U: a physical 8 GiB card (G8: Windows over-commits and pages instead of failing; the definition is measured, not enforced); other models, CPUs and drivers; contexts above 24k; the prefetch's precision on prompts other than the two captured (the offline screen used code and prose; the held-out anchor prompt was not measured with the prefetch); the pipelined launch (ledger row 34, +9..11% estimated) is designed, not built.
   - M: process notes. 69 GPU sessions since the previous summary; 10 were recorded UNCERTIFIED by the provenance gate and none is cited as evidence (stale binaries after a header edit, a literal `$B` in a path); no criterion was moved after a result (misses: G13-1 strict reading, G16-1's baseline clause in its first session, G17c-1 / G17c-2), every miss is reported.
5. **Recommendation (autonomous acceptance, not owner approval).** **Adopt** the final configuration as the TG path for the 8 GB / 262k shape on this machine: `llama-layer-streaming ... --pp-schedule batch-major --ubatch 24 --pp-no-logits --kv-graph-pad 256 --kv-lazy --tg-release-compute --tg-fast-sample --tg-hybrid --tg-hybrid-slots auto --tg-vram-cap-mib 0 --tg-vram-keep-free-mib 16000` with `LLAMA_MOE_HYBRID=14 LLAMA_MOE_HYBRID_B=16 LLAMA_MOE_HYBRID_FREQ=<aer-freq-train.txt> LLAMA_CUDA_INPUT_BATCH=1 LLAMA_MOE_HYBRID_SCAN=2 LLAMA_MOE_HYBRID_LEAN=1 LLAMA_MOE_HYBRID_LEAN_PF=2` (the exact command lines are in the arm records of `g37-final-code`); add `--mtp --mtp-draft 1 --mtp-draft-experts-gpu` for predictable output (code, structured text), leave it out for prose. **Keep experimenting** only if the owner wants the last 10% (ledger row 34, the pipelined launch). **Decide / validate next:** (a) whether 17.5 GiB of pinned RAM and 8 spinning threads are acceptable and whether the machine-specific placement defaults should be generalised (a topology probe); (b) the nested clone's commit policy (its `AGENTS.md` demands per-commit human approval; the work there stays uncommitted and is recorded in the regenerated patch); (c) the doc-size debt that makes `tools/run-tests.ps1 -Suite host` fail its size guard; (d) **a run on a physical 8 GiB card** before anyone quotes "fits 8 GiB" as a hardware fact; (e) pushes and upstream material (none needed). Decision log: D1-D60 in this file; contracts G1-G18 and their results above; acceptance scope: each acceptance certifies only what its contract defines.

## OWNER SUMMARY (2026-10-11 after G1-G7; superseded by the 2026-10-12 summary above for the numbers, kept for the history of the 69-81 slot configurations)
1. **Original task / goal / idea explored.** Beat the token-generation speed of `docs/llamacpp 8gb vram 262k base.md` (30.68 tok/s, `llama-server`, 8 GB VRAM, 262,144-token window) with caching and scheduling that raise GPU utilisation, prove every idea
   or disprove it with its root cause, and never dismiss a good idea because of a bad implementation. Idea tested: keep the experts used recently in a per-layer VRAM cache, compute the misses on the CPU from RAM, and spend every byte of VRAM that other parts do not need on that cache.
2. **How it works, in simple terms.** The model has 40 layers; each holds 256 small expert networks and the router picks 8 per token. A stock run with 37 layers' experts in system RAM does all of that expert math on the CPU, token after token. The hybrid keeps the recently
   used experts of every layer in VRAM. For each token and layer the GPU runs the cached experts it needs and posts the missed ones to a shared mailbox; 8 CPU threads read the same weights from RAM, compute the misses, and write the result back; the GPU adds it in. Between tokens a running
   score swaps hot experts into the cache by background copies. Arithmetic and routing are unchanged. Seven things made it fast and safe: (a) the attention window is sized to the live tokens, not to the 262,144 slots (`--kv-graph-pad`); (b) prompt activations stay f32 (bf16 made the
   output degenerate); (c) the cache is resized after the prompt, when the prompt's weight staging is gone (`--tg-hybrid-slots`); (d) the prompt's scratch buffers are released first (`--tg-release-compute`); (e) the 262,144-token KV cache is only RESERVED as address space and the
   rows a run touches are committed (`--kv-lazy`), which frees 2.6 GiB for the cache; (f) the engine thread never makes a CUDA call, because the only crash found was a three-way deadlock (GPU spin-wait, the launching thread's driver lock, the engine's copy submission); (g) optionally the
   ten small per-token uploads are batched (`LLAMA_CUDA_INPUT_BATCH=1`, +3.1%).
3. **Glossary.** TG = token generation (decode, one token per step); PP = prompt processing (prefill); tok/s = tokens per second. KV cache = the attention keys/values kept for every earlier token (here q8, 10 attention layers); a *row* = one token's slice of it.
   MoE = mixture of experts; an expert is one feed-forward block, 256 per layer, the router selects 8. VRAM = GPU memory. Slots / C42 / C82 = how many experts per layer the VRAM cache holds (42, 82). Hit rate = the share of a token's selected experts found in the VRAM cache.
   HYB = the hybrid with the default 14 slots, HYB28 = resized to 28, HYB42R = 42 slots after releasing the prompt scratch buffers, HYB42RB = that plus input batching, L82 = HYB42R with the lazily committed KV cache and 82 slots, L82B = L82 plus input batching, AUTO = cache size picked by the runner,
   STK = stock `llama-completion` with the owner's flags (37 layers of experts on the CPU). CUDA graph = a recorded GPU launch replayed each token; the *pad boundary* = every 256 live tokens the attention window grows and the graph is rebuilt and re-recorded. Engine thread = the thread
   that serves the CPU misses; copy thread = the thread that submits the background cache-fill copies. Driver lock = the lock the GPU driver takes for calls such as memory allocation or loading a kernel for the first time. VMM / lazy commit = CUDA virtual memory management: reserve address
   space now, back it with real VRAM only when needed. Poller = the 50 ms `nvidia-smi` loop whose maximum is every "whole-GPU peak" quoted here; NVML = the driver's management library, `cudaMemGetInfo` = the CUDA runtime's own free-memory call (both read differently, see the caveat in G7 RESULTS).
   QA gate = a programmatic needle test (the answer must contain the planted codes; a negative control must fail); answer stability, never token identity, is what it certifies.
4. **Pros and cons** (M = measured, E = expected/derived, U = unproven; evidence under `build/llama-cuda/<session>/` and the named plan sections).
   - M: **final build, same machine, interleaved reps in one mutex hold:** stock 39.05 / 38.73 / 38.84 tok/s (code / prose / anchor) against hybrid L82 103.80 / 106.26 / 102.34 (**2.66x / 2.74x / 2.64x**) and L82B 107.73 / 110.92 / 106.47 (**2.76x / 2.86x / 2.74x**, 3.5x the owner's 30.68). Without lazy KV (HYB42R) 86.94 / 89.35 / 77.85 = 2.2x. (`g11-code`, `g11-prose`, `g11-anchor`, G3/G4/G6 results.)
   - M: answer stability: needle QA passes (negative control fails as designed) at 511 tokens for every hybrid arm and for stock, and at 4,096 and 24,000 prompt tokens with lazy C78; the text at equal slot counts is byte-identical with and without lazy KV, with and without batching, with and without the copy thread. Between different cache sizes the text parts company at near-tie steps (dlogit median 0.10-0.22). (`g11-anchor`, `g10-qa*`, `g10-lazy`.)
   - M: reliability: 104 hybrid runs in the final-build sessions, 0 failures; the one defect found (3 crashes in about 500 earlier runs) is root-caused, reproduced 16 of 16 and fixed 16 of 16 (G5). The idea ledger lists 34 ideas with their mechanisms and measured causes (rows 20-25, 32 and 23 are the disproven or bounded ones of this round).
   - M: costs: about 17.5 GiB of pinned system RAM (the rig has 62 GB); 8 CPU threads busy-waiting at high priority during generation; with all 32 logical CPUs busy the speed collapses to 0.35 tok/s (4 engine threads: 34 tok/s); PP about 9% slower than stock at 511 tokens (317 vs 349 tok/s, G1); a one-time ~2 s warm-up when the model's experts are first touched.
   - M: **the VRAM claim is a proxy.** "Fits 8 GiB" means the nvidia-smi poller's whole-GPU maximum is at most 8,192 MiB on a 24 GiB card, the same metric the stock command is judged by (7,435). The CUDA runtime's own view of the same moment reads 1,080 MiB higher, so in that view C82 would not fit; the configuration that does (auto at the runtime cap, 65 slots) runs 100.7 tok/s,
     still 2.6x stock. A physical 8 GiB card was not available; its driver reserve is unknown. (G7 RESULTS, VRAM ACCOUNTING CAVEAT.)
   - M/E: lazy KV helps in proportion to the unfilled window: measured at 1.1k, 4k and 25k committed rows, derived beyond (0.0146 cache slots per MiB of committed KV; a full 262k window gains nothing). The speed at long contexts, with attention reading the whole live window, is U.
   - M: not accepted as written (criteria not moved): G4 input batching (+3.1% real, but the gap-idle drop was -299 us against the required -400) and G7 auto-sizing (works, never over the cap, but chose 76 slots against the 79-82 band). Both stay opt-in.
   - U: other models, GPUs and drivers; contexts above 24k tokens; the CPU power and thermal cost of the spinning threads; a verified run on a physical 8 GiB card. Every arm ran with `--ignore-eos`, so the text after the answer is junk in all engines.
5. **Recommendation (autonomous acceptance, not owner approval).** The goal as written is met and exceeded, and the accepted contracts G1, G2, G3, G5 and G6 certify exactly the claims in their acceptance sections. **Adopt** the hybrid with `--kv-graph-pad 256 --tg-hybrid --tg-first-pp-token --tg-release-compute --kv-lazy --tg-hybrid-slots N`
   (82 on this machine and prompt shape; `auto` as the safe default) and the copy thread (default on) as the TG path for the 8 GB shape; keep input batching opt-in. **Decide / validate next:** (a) whether 17.5 GiB of pinned RAM and 8 spinning threads are acceptable; (b) the nested clone's commit policy (its `AGENTS.md` demands per-commit human approval; the work there is uncommitted);
   (c) the doc-size debt that makes `tools/run-tests.ps1 -Suite host` fail its size guard; (d) `--tg-first-pp-token` as the default; (e) pushes and upstream material (none needed so far); (f) **a run on a physical 8 GiB card, or at least the runtime-view cap, before anyone quotes "fits 8 GiB" as a hardware fact**; (g) whether a VMM-backed KV buffer is wanted outside this experiment. Decision log: D1-D41 in this file.

- D12 Fit to the 8 GiB shape by slot count, measuring VRAM with a whole-GPU poller (E20): C14 is the largest that fits.
- D13 Accept the kv-pad fix as part of the hybrid path (it is opt-in `--kv-graph-pad`; default behaviour unchanged).
